The IMPRIMITIVITY FELL BUNDLE
Anna Duwenig

Abstract
. Given a full right-Hilbert C ∗ \mathrm{C}^{*} -module 𝐗 \mathbf{X} over a C ∗ \mathrm{C}^{*} -algebra A A , the set 𝕂 A ⁡ ( 𝐗 ) \compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X}) of A A -compact operators on 𝐗 \mathbf{X} is the (up to isomorphism) unique C ∗ \mathrm{C}^{*} -algebra that is strongly Morita equivalent to the coefficient algebra A A via 𝐗 \mathbf{X} . As bimodule, 𝕂 A ⁡ ( 𝐗 ) \compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X}) can also be thought of as the balanced tensor product 𝐗 ⊗ A 𝐗 op \mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{op}} , and so the latter naturally becomes a C ∗ \mathrm{C}^{*} -algebra. We generalize both of these facts to the world of Fell bundles over groupoids: Suppose ℬ \mathscr{B} is a Fell bundle over a groupoid ℋ \mathcal{H} and ℳ \mathscr{M} an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle over a principal ℋ \mathcal{H} -space X X . If ℳ \mathscr{M} carries a right-action of ℬ \mathscr{B} and a sufficiently nice ℬ \mathscr{B} -valued inner product, then its imprimitivity Fell bundle 𝕂 ℬ ⁡ ( ℳ ) = ℳ ⊗ ℬ ℳ op \compacts_{\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{M})=\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}} is a Fell bundle over the imprimitivity groupoid of X X , and it is the unique Fell bundle that is equivalent to ℬ \mathscr{B} via ℳ \mathscr{M} . We show that 𝕂 ℬ ⁡ ( ℳ ) \compacts_{\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{M}) generalizes the ‘higher order’ compact operators of Abadie–Ferraro in the case of saturated bundles over groups, and that the theorem recovers results such as Kumjian’s Stabilization trick.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
46L55, 46L05, 22A22

1.
Introduction

Suppose a groupoid
\mathcal{H}
acts on a topological space
X
, meaning that we have a continuous open surjection
\sigma\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
(the
anchor map
of the action) and a continuous map

(1.1)

X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\{(x,h)\in X\times\mathcal{H}:\sigma(x)=r_{\mathcal{H}}(h)\}\to X,\quad(x,h)\mapsto x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h.

Let us further assume that
X
and
\mathcal{H}
are locally compact Hausdorff, and that the action is free and proper (i.e.,
X
is a
principal (right)
\mathcal{H}
-space
;
[
MRW:Grpd
, p. 6]
).
Out of
X
, we can build a
left

\mathcal{H}
-space
X^{\mathrm{op}}
as follows: as a topological space, it is just
X
, but we write its elements with a superscript-op to avoid confusion. Its left action uses the anchor map
\sigma^{\mathrm{op}}\colon x^{\mathrm{op}}\mapsto\sigma(x)
, and the action is given by
h\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}x^{\mathrm{op}}=(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}
for any
h
with
s_{\mathcal{H}}(h)=\sigma^{\mathrm{op}}(x^{\mathrm{op}})
. Whenever
\sigma^{\mathrm{op}}
appears in a subscript, we will drop its superscript and simply write
\sigma
.
Now consider

X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}=\{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\in X\times X^{\mathrm{op}}:\sigma(x)=\sigma^{\mathrm{op}}(y^{\mathrm{op}})\}.

With the subspace topology of the product topology,
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
is locally compact Hausdorff.
For any given
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\in X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
, consider the orbit

[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\coloneqq\{(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y^{\mathrm{op}}):h\in\sigma(x)\mathcal{H}\}

of
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
under the ‘diagonal’
\mathcal{H}
-action on
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
. Since

\mathcal{H}
is assumed to have open source map
and to
act properly on
X
,
it follows that the quotient
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
by this equivalence relation is also a locally compact Hausdorff space
[
28
, Proposition 2.18]
, and likewise is the quotient
X/\mathcal{H}
.

We equip
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
with the structure of a groupoid with unit space
X/\mathcal{H}
: its source and range maps are given by

s[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]=[y,y^{\mathrm{op}}]=r[y,z^{\mathrm{op}}],

and we can identify
[y,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
with
y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathcal{H}\in X/\mathcal{H}
.
Given
\xi,\eta\in X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
with
s(\xi)=r(\eta)
, we can find representatives
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and
(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})
of
\xi
respectively
\eta
with matching component ‘in the middle’, and then define their product by
\xi\eta=[x,z^{\mathrm{op}}]
.

The groupoid
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
acts on the left of
X
: we define the anchor map

(1.2)

\rho\colon X\to(X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}})^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}\cong X/\mathcal{H}\quad\text{ by }\quad\rho(x)=[x,x^{\mathrm{op}}]\triangleeq x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathcal{H}.

Note that
\rho
is
open
[
28
, Proposition 2.12.]
and continuous. If
(\xi,y)\in(X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}){\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X
, then
s(\xi)=\rho(y)
implies that there exists
x\in X
such that
\xi=[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
; this
x
is unique by freeness of the
\mathcal{H}
-action on
X
. We can therefore define
\xi\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y=x
, i.e.,

(1.3)

X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}\curvearrowright X\colon\qquad[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y=x.

Remark 1.1
.

Suppose
X
is a
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
-groupoid equivalence (see
[
MRW:Grpd
]
) with anchor maps
\sigma\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
and
\rho\colon X\to\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
.
If
\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)
, then there exists a unique element
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
in
\mathcal{G}
such that
x=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y
. Indeed, since
\sigma
is assumed to induce a homeomorphism
\tilde{\sigma}\colon\mathcal{G}\backslash X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
, the equality
\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)
means exactly that
\mathcal{G}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}x=\mathcal{G}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y
, and so freeness of the
\mathcal{G}
-action implies the existence of the unique
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
which transforms
y
into
x
. Since the action is proper and
\rho
is open, the surjective map
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-26.39096pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 23.10555pt}}_{{\kern-25.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 23.10555pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle is continuous and open, and it factors through the quotient
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X
of
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
by the diagonal
\mathcal{H}
-action, yielding a homeomorphism
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathcal{G}
.

Likewise, if
\rho(x)=\rho(y)
, we write
\mathchoice{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}
for the unique element of
\mathcal{H}
such that
x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathchoice{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}=y
, and
\mathchoice{\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}\colon X^{\mathrm{op}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.49164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X\to\mathcal{H}
induces a homeomorphism
X^{\mathrm{op}}\times_{\mathcal{G}}X\cong\mathcal{H}
. A quick computation shows that the following equalities hold (wherever one side of the equation makes sense):

(1.4)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}z

\displaystyle=x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathchoice{\left\{y^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty z\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{y^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty z\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{y^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty z\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{y^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty z\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}

(1.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-53.6645pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 50.37909pt}}_{{\kern-53.23672pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 50.37909pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle Example 1.2
.

Any groupoid
\mathcal{H}
acts freely and properly on
X=\mathcal{H}
; say, on the right. The anchor map is then given by
\sigma=s_{\mathcal{H}}\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
, and the associated imprimitivity groupoid
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{H}\times_{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{op}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{H}
via
f\colon[h_{1},h_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto h_{1}h_{2}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}
. In particular, the map
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-26.39096pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 23.10555pt}}_{{\kern-25.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 23.10555pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-26.39096pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 23.10555pt}}_{{\kern-25.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 23.10555pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern becomes the map
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{H},(h_{1},h_{2})\mapsto h_{1}h_{2}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}
. This isomorphism further turns the anchor map
\rho\colon X\to\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}},h\mapsto[h,h^{\mathrm{op}}],
into the range map
r_{\mathcal{H}}\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
, and
\mathchoice{\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern .

We arrive at a well-known result:

Lemma 1.3

(motivation;
[
28
, Lemma 2.45, Proposition 2.47]
)
.

Suppose
X
is a principal
\mathcal{H}
-space. Then
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
is a locally compact Hausdorff groupoid with open source map that acts freely and properly on the left of
X
with anchor map
\rho\colon x\mapsto[x,x^{\mathrm{op}}]
. With this structure,
X
is a
(X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{H})
-equivalence of groupoids. Moreover, if
X
is also a
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
-equivalence, then there exists a groupoid isomorphism
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{G}
that is uniquely determined by
[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
.

Note that the above in particular states that, in the setting where
\mathcal{G}=X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
, the element

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
of
\mathcal{G}
for
x,y\in Xu

is

[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
.

A reason why one might be interested in groupoid equivalences is the following result due to works by
[
21
]
.

Lemma 1.4

(
[
30
]
,
[
21
]
)
.

Suppose
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}
are locally compact Hausdorff groupoids, that
\{\lambda_{u}\}_{u\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}
is a Haar system on
\mathcal{H}
, and that there exists an equivalence
X
between
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
(with open anchor maps). Then
\mathcal{G}
also allows a Haar system, and if say
\{\mu_{v}\}_{v\in\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}
is any such Haar system, then the groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{G},\mu)
and
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{H},\lambda)
are strongly Morita equivalent via an imprimitivity bimodule built as a completion of
C_{c}(X)
.

An analogous theorem holds for the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra of equivalent Fell bundles
[
22
]
. Such results are powerful, for example because two strongly Morita equivalent
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras have the same representation theory,
\operatorname{K}
-theory, and lattices of ideals (via the so-called “Rieffel Correspondence”). But not only do the above mentioned results state the existence of a strong Morita equivalence, they also
construct it explicitly
, which allows one to, for example, construct representatives of certain famous classes in
\operatorname{KK}
-theory
[
3
]
.

There is a statement analogous to Lemma
1.3
for a right Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module
\mathbf{X}
over
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
. In the following, we use the symbol
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.59999pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 11.44585pt}}_{{\kern-14.18196pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 11.44585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.59999pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 11.44585pt}}_{{\kern-14.18196pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 11.44585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-12.79483pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 9.64069pt}}_{{\kern-12.3768pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 9.64069pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.94511pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 8.79097pt}}_{{\kern-11.52708pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 8.79097pt}}}\left\langle\mathbf{y}\right\rvert
for
\mathbf{x,y}\in\mathbf{X}
to denote the ‘
A
-rank-one’ operator
\mathbf{X}\to\mathbf{X}
that maps
\mathbf{z}
to
\mathbf{x}\cdot{\mathchoice{\langle\mathbf{y}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{z}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{y}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{z}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{y}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{z}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{y}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{z}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}}\in\mathbf{X}
, and we let
\compacts(\mathbf{X}_{A})=\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})
denote the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra generated by these operators; it is an ideal of the
A
-adjointable operators (see
[
RaWi:Morita
, Lemma 2.25]
). We get that
\mathbf{X}
has a
left

\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})
-inner product given by
1

With inner products such as these, we will drop the sub- and/or superscripts whenever there is no ambiguity. Furthermore, we let
\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
be the dual, left-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module as defined in
[
24
, p. 49]
, meaning there exists an additive bijection
F\colon\mathbf{X}\to\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
such that
F(\lambda\mathbf{x})=\bar{\lambda}F(\mathbf{x})
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{X}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.
The analogue can now be stated as:

Lemma 1.5

(motivation;
[
24
, Proposition 2.21, Lemma 2.25, Proposition 3.8.]
)
.

Suppose
\mathbf{X}
is a full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module over a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
.
Then the set
\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})
of
A
-compact operators on
\mathbf{X}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra with respect to the operator norm, and
\mathbf{X}
is a
(\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X}),A)
-imprimitivity bimodule. Moreover, if
\mathbf{X}
is also a
(B,A)
-imprimitivity bimodule, then there exists a *-isomorphism
\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})\to B
that is uniquely determined by
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.59999pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 11.44585pt}}_{{\kern-14.18196pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 11.44585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.59999pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 11.44585pt}}_{{\kern-14.18196pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 11.44585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-12.79483pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 9.64069pt}}_{{\kern-12.3768pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 9.64069pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.94511pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 8.79097pt}}_{{\kern-11.52708pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 8.79097pt}}}\left\langle\mathbf{y}\right\rvert\mapsto{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B}}}}\langle\mathbf{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}\rangle^{{\kern-24.06804pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 19.28056pt}}_{{\kern-22.22205pt{\scriptscriptstyle B}\kern 19.28056pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B}}}}\langle\mathbf{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}\rangle^{{\kern-24.06804pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 19.28056pt}}_{{\kern-22.22205pt{\scriptscriptstyle B}\kern 19.28056pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B}}}}\langle\mathbf{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}\rangle^{{\kern-19.13548pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 14.81468pt}}_{{\kern-17.75616pt{\scriptscriptstyle B}\kern 14.81468pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B}}}}\langle\mathbf{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}\rangle^{{\kern-17.12494pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 12.80414pt}}_{{\kern-15.74562pt{\scriptscriptstyle B}\kern 12.80414pt}}}}
.

Let us elaborate on the analogy to Lemma
1.3
:
If
\mathbf{Y}
is another full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module over
A
, then the map

\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{X})
determined by
\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{op}}\mapsto\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.59999pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 11.44585pt}}_{{\kern-14.18196pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 11.44585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.59999pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 11.44585pt}}_{{\kern-14.18196pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 11.44585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-12.79483pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 9.64069pt}}_{{\kern-12.3768pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 9.64069pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.94511pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}\kern 8.79097pt}}_{{\kern-11.52708pt{\scriptscriptstyle A}\kern 8.79097pt}}}\left\langle\mathbf{y}\right\rvert
is an isomorphism of bi-Hilbert
\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})-\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{Y})
-bimodules (see Lemma
A.2
). In particular,
\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})
is, in fact, the (up to isomorphism) unique
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra that is equivalent to
A
via
\mathbf{X}
. (Note that we similarly have
\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes_{\compacts}\mathbf{X}\cong A
as bi-Hilbert
A-A
-modules via
\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{2}\mapsto{\mathchoice{\langle\mathbf{x}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{x}_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{x}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{x}_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{x}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{x}_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{x}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{x}_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{X}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}}
.)

The main result of this paper, Theorem
1.6
, is an analogue of Lemma
1.3
and Proposition
1.5
in the setting of Fell bundles over groupoids. To this end, we will first show that the correct analogue of a principal
\mathcal{H}
-space and a full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module over
A
is a
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence
, which can be thought of as “half” of a Fell bundle equivalence in the sense of
[
22
, Def. 6.1]
. We will then prove:

Theorem 1.6
.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
is a locally compact Hausdorff groupoid with open source map and
X
is a principal
\mathcal{H}
-space with open anchor map. If
\mathscr{B}
is a saturated Fell bundle over
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathscr{M}
is a
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence over
X
, then there is a saturated Fell bundle
\compacts(\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{B}})
over the imprimitivity groupoid of
X
that is equivalent to
\mathscr{B}
via
\mathscr{M}
. Moreover, if
\mathscr{M}
is also an
(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})
-Fell bundle equivalence, then there exists a Fell bundle isomorphism
\compacts(\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{B}})\to\mathscr{A}
that is uniquely determined by
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-17.08224pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-16.35309pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-17.08224pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-16.35309pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-14.28839pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 10.96895pt}}_{{\kern-13.55923pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\kern 10.96895pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.05917pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 9.73973pt}}_{{\kern-12.33002pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}\kern 9.73973pt}}}\left\langle n\right\rvert\mapsto{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-28.13977pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-28.13977pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-24.23816pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 19.75206pt}}_{{\kern-22.48817pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 19.75206pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-21.8575pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 17.3714pt}}_{{\kern-20.10751pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 17.3714pt}}}}
.

Many theorems about symmetric imprimitivity
[
7
,
16
,
13
,
5
,
14
]
and generalized fixed point algebras
[
25
,
8
,
10
]
have appeared over the years and are extending (in various directions) a
[
11
]
-result of
[
11
]
’s

[
11
]
.
In Section
6
, we will see in what way our theorem captures some of those result.

The structure of the paper is as follows.
After establishing notation and assumptions in Subsection
1.1
, we give the definition of a
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence
\mathscr{M}
over a principal
\mathcal{H}
-space
X
(Definition
2.1
) and prove some of its basic properties in Section
2
. Section
3
is devoted to proving that
\mathscr{M}
gives rise to a Fell bundle
\compacts(\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{B}})
over the imprimitivity groupoid of
X
, which we will also denote by
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
for reasons that will become apparent. We prove that this
imprimitivity Fell bundle
is equivalent to
\mathscr{B}
via
\mathscr{M}
in Section
4
. In Section
5
, we prove that
\compacts(\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{B}})
is unique (up to isomorphism), and we see some applications in Section
6
. There are two short appendices to establish some background results about Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules and about upper semi-continuous Banach bundles.

1.1.
Assumptions, conventions, and notation

We will denote groupoids using
\mathcal
(
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\ldots
), bundles using
\mathscr
(
\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B},\mathscr{C},\ldots
), and stand-alone Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules and their elements using
\mathbf
(
\mathbf{x\in X,y\in Y,z\in Z}\ldots
). Fibred products are denoted by
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.11961pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.11961pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.47238pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.77238pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
, as defined in (
1.1
). The algebraic tensor product is
\odot
and its completions is
\otimes
; we add a subscript for the internal (i.e., balanced) tensor product. Right and left actions on spaces are usually denoted by
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
and
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
, respectively, while actions on bundles are denoted by
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
and
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
. (The triangle is always pointing at the object that is being acted on.)

We try to use the letter
p
for the projection map of Fell bundles, while those of general Banach bundles are denoted by
q
. The fibre over a point
h
of the base space of a bundle
\mathscr{B}=(B\to\mathcal{H})
with total space
B
is denoted
B(h)
; we adopt analogous notation for other bundles. We write
s_{\mathscr{B}}\colon B\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
for the source map of
\mathcal{H}
composed with the projection map of
\mathscr{B}
, and we adopt analogous notation for anchor maps
\sigma\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
.

We use the notion of upper semi-continuous Banach bundles, Fell bundles, actions of Fell bundles on upper semi-continuous Banach bundles, and equivalences of Fell bundles as in
[
4
]
, Definitions 2.3, 2.9, 2.10, and 2.11, respectively.
As done in
[
22
]
, our Fell bundles are assumed
saturated
in the sense that their fibres are full (and hence imprimitivity) bimodules; we will rarely mention this assumption again. We frequently use the fact that our upper semi-continuous Banach bundles have enough continuous cross sections (see
[
18
, Cor. 2.10]
,
[
22
, App. A]
).

\blacktriangleright

For the remainder of the paper
, we assume that

•

\mathcal{H}
is a locally compact Hausdorff groupoid with open source map
2
2

2

Any locally compact Hausdorff groupoid with a Haar system (and thus any étale groupoid) is an example of a groupoid with open source map
[
28
, Proposition 1.23]
.

s_{\mathcal{H}}\colon\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
,

•

X
is a locally compact Hausdorff space and
\sigma\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
is a continuous open surjection,

•

X
is a principal right
\mathcal{H}
-space with anchor map
\sigma
, and

•

the groupoid
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
will be denoted by
\mathcal{G}
, and we will refer to it as the
imprimitivity groupoid
of
X
as in
[
28
, Lemma 2.45]
.

In analogy to the notation
\mathcal{H}u=s_{\mathcal{H}}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(u)
and
u\mathcal{H}=r_{\mathcal{H}}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(u)
for
u\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
, we will write
Xu\coloneqq\sigma^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(u)
. We chose the letter
\sigma
since it serves the purpose of a ‘source’ map; for left-actions, we will therefore generally use
\rho
(as in ‘range’) for the anchor map.

2.
Demi-Equivalences

For the upcoming definition, we remind the reader that the anchor map
\rho\colon X\to\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
of the left action of
\mathcal{G}=X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
on the principal
\mathcal{H}
-space
X
is exactly the quotient map if we identify
\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
with
X/\mathcal{H}
; see (
1.2
).

Definition 2.1

(cf.
[

, Definition 2.1]
)
.

Suppose
\mathscr{B}=(p_{\mathscr{B}}\colon B\to\mathcal{H})

{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
covers the map
\mathchoice{\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}
in the sense that

q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1})\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}p_{\mathscr{B}}\bigl({\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\bigr)=q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})
;

(DE3)

{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
is continuous, and fibrewise sesquilinear (meaning linear in the second and anti-linear in the first coordinate);

(DE4)

{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}b
;

(DE5)

{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
;

(DE6)

{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\geq 0
in the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
B(\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m))
, and
{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}=0
only if
m=0
;

(DE7)

the norm
m\mapsto\left\|{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|^{1/2}
agrees with the norm that the upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{M}
carries; and

(DE8)

for each
x\in X
, the linear span of
\{{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}:m_{i}\in M(x)\}
is dense in
B(\sigma(x))
.

An analogous definition of a left
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence can be made; there, each instance of a range map becomes a source map (and vice versa) and sesquilinearity means that the
first
coordinate is linear.
When there is no ambiguity, we will drop the subscript-
\mathscr{B}
on the inner product; conversely, when there is ample room for ambiguity, we might add a superscript-
\mathscr{M}
.

The reader might have noticed that there are a few natural properties, both algebraic and analytic, that a demi-equivalence should satisfy if it is to be ‘half’ of an equivalence in the sense of
[
22
]
. Let us show that all those properties actually follow automatically:

Lemma 2.2

(cf.
[
1
, Lemma 2.7]
)
.

Suppose
\mathscr{B}=(p_{\mathscr{B}}\colon B\to\mathcal{H})
is a Fell bundle over the groupoid
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathscr{M}=(q_{\mathscr{M}}\colon M\to X)
is a right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence over the principal right
\mathcal{H}
-space
X
. Then we have the following, where
(m_{1},m_{2})\in M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.19164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.49164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
.

(DE9)

Each
M(x)
is a full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module over the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
B(\sigma(x))
;

(DE10)

{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}=b{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
for all appropriate
b\in B
;

(DE11)

\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1})=r_{\mathscr{B}}({\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})
and
\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})=s_{\mathscr{B}}({\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})
;

(DE12)

{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle*}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\leq\left\|m_{2}\right\|^{2}{\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}

as elements of the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
B(\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1}))

(
Cauchy–Schwarz
)
.

Moreover,
\mathscr{B}
acts on the right of
\mathscr{M}
in the sense of
[
4
, Definition 2.11]
, i.e.,

(DE13)

\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}
is fibrewise bilinear;

(DE14)

(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{\prime}=m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(bb^{\prime})
for all appropriate
b,b^{\prime}\in B
;

(DE15)

\left\|m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\right\|\leq\left\|m\right\|\left\|b\right\|
; and

(DE16)

\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}
is continuous.

Again, the analogous result for a left demi-equivalence must be rephrased in a way that every range becomes a source etc.
With the properties listed in Lemma
2.2
, we see that a
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence really satisfies all ‘one-sided’ properties of a Fell bundle equivalence as defined in
[
22
, Def. 6.1]
. In Proposition
2.4
, we will see which properties are needed for a ‘two-sided’ demi-equivalence to be an equivalence.

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

Condition
(DE9)
is just a restatement of other properties. To be precise,
M(x)
is a right inner product
B(\sigma(x))
-module in the sense of
[
RaWi:Morita
, Definition 2.1]
because of Conditions
(DE3)
–
(DE6)
, and it is a Hilbert
B(\sigma(x))
-module because we assumed that the norm with respect to which
M(x)
is complete coincides with the norm induced by the inner product (Assumption
(DE7)
). Fullness is exactly Assumption
(DE8)
.

Condition
(DE10)
follows from
(DE5)
and
(DE4)
combined, and
(DE11)
follows directly from
(DE2)
.

(DE13)
Fix
x\in X
and
m,m_{i}\in M(x)
. If
b\in B(h)
for some
h\in\sigma(x)\mathcal{H}
, then

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\ref{item:rwordBdl:ip:C*linear}}{=}{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}+\lambda m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}+\lambda m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}+\lambda m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}+\lambda m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}}b

\displaystyle=({\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}}+\lambda{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}})b

\displaystyle\text{ by \ref{item:rwordBdl:ip} (sesquilinearity)}.

Since multiplication in
\mathscr{B}
is bilinear, we conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty(m_{1}+\lambda m_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b+\lambda(m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b+\lambda(m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b+\lambda(m_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}}

for all
m\in M(x)
. Choosing
