, it follows from
(DE6)
that
m=0
, meaning that
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
is linear in the first component. A similar computation, using
(DE10)
, proves linearity in the second component and also
(DE14)
, using associativity of the multiplication of
\mathscr{B}
.

(DE15)
We have

\left\|m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\right\|^{2}\overset{\ref{item:rwordBdl:norm compatible}}{=}\left\|{\mathchoice{\langle m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\penalty\mid\penalty m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\penalty\mid\penalty m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\penalty\mid\penalty m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|\overset{(\dagger)}{=}\left\|b^{*}{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|\left\|b\right\|\overset{\ref{item:rwordBdl:norm compatible}}{=}\left\|m\right\|^{2}\left\|b\right\|^{2},

where

follows from

and
(DE10)
.

(DE16)
Suppose
(m_{i},b_{i})
is a net in
M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}B
that converges to
(m,b)
; in particular,
x_{i}\coloneqq q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{i})
converges to
x\coloneqq q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)
in
X
and
h_{i}\coloneqq p_{\mathscr{B}}(b_{i})
converges to
h\coloneqq p_{\mathscr{B}}(b)
in
\mathcal{H}
. Now choose a continuous section
\mu\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})
of
\mathscr{M}
with
\mu(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)=m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b
. Since
\mu
and the right
\mathcal{H}
-action
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
on
X
are continuous, we have
\mu(x_{i}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{i})\to m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b
. Using
(DE4)
,
(DE10)
, and sesquilinearity of the inner product, we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\langle m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{i}-\mu(x_{i}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{i})\penalty\mid\penalty m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{i}-\mu(x_{i}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{i})\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}}{\langle m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{i}-\mu(x_{i}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{i})\penalty\mid\penalty m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{i}-\mu(x_{i}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{i})\penalty\mid\penalty The proof of

is verbatim that for

, Lemma 4.7]
(after translating from the left to the right).
∎

Remark 2.3
.

In
[
22
, Definition 6.1]
, neither continuity of the inner product on a Fell bundle equivalence nor Condition
(DE7)
were explicitly assumed but often invoked. I am unsure whether there is a way to deduce continuity from a combination of the algebraic properties of
\mathscr{M}
and the topological properties of
\mathscr{B}
, like it was the case for continuity of the action,
(DE16)
. Consequently, I chose to include these conditions as assumptions in the definition.

Note that a two-sided version of a demi-equivalence is an equivalence in the sense of
[
22
, Definition 6.1]
, provided there is some algebraic compatibility between the two structures:

Proposition 2.4
.

Suppose that
X
is a groupoid equivalence between
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
with anchor maps
\rho
respectively
\sigma
, that
\mathscr{A}=(A\to\mathcal{G})
and
\mathscr{B}=(B\to\mathcal{H})
are Fell bundles, and that
\mathscr{M}=(M\to X)
is a left
\mathscr{A}
- and a right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence. Assume further that

(1)

the actions of
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{B}
on
\mathscr{M}
commute, i.e., for all
(a,m,b)\in A{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}B
, we have
(a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b=a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)
;

(2)

for each
x\in X
,
A(\rho(x))
acts by
B(\sigma(x))
-adjointable operators on
M(x)
, meaning that

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty a^{*}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty a^{*}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}

for all
m_{i}\in M(x)
and all
a\in A(\rho(x))
;

(3)

for all
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\in M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.19164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.49164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
, the inner products on
\mathscr{M}
satisfy

{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-25.06996pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 23.79219pt}}_{{\kern-26.5283pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 23.79219pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-22.30042pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.02264pt}}_{{\kern-23.75876pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.02264pt}}}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{3}=m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}.

Then
\mathscr{M}
is an equivalence between
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{B}
.

Note that Assumption
(2)
is distinctly asymmetric; it could have been replaced by its counterpart:

(2)
’

For each
x\in X
,
B(\sigma(x))
acts by
A(\rho(x))
-adjointable operators on
M(x)
.

Proof.

First, we will do some sanity checks: Condition
(1)
makes sense because the actions on
X
commute, meaning that
(a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b
and
a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)
live over the same fibre of
\mathscr{M}
by
(DE1)
.
Next, let us check that Condition
(3)
makes sense.
Since
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-22.62776pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 20.88332pt}}_{{\kern-23.61943pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 20.88332pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-22.62776pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 20.88332pt}}_{{\kern-23.61943pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 20.88332pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-17.00441pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.72664pt}}_{{\kern-18.46275pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 15.72664pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-14.7333pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.45554pt}}_{{\kern-16.19165pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 13.45554pt}}}}
is defined on
M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
and
{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
on
M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.19164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.49164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
, we can evaluate the shown inner products. By
(DE11)
, we have

\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})=r_{\mathscr{B}}({\mathchoice{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})
,
and since
(m_{1},m_{2})\in M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
, we therefore have that
m_{1}
can be acted on by
{\mathchoice{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{2}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{3}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
on the right; a similar argument shows that
m_{3}
can be acted on by
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-25.06996pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 23.79219pt}}_{{\kern-26.5283pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 23.79219pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-22.30042pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.02264pt}}_{{\kern-23.75876pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.02264pt}}}}
on the left. Lastly, note that Conditions
(DE1)
and
(DE2)
combined with Equation (
1.4
) show that the elements in question are indeed living in the same fibre of
\mathscr{M}
.

To see that
\mathscr{M}
is an equivalence, we have to check Properties (FE1), (FE2), and (FE3) in
[
4
, Definition 2.11]
. By assumption,
\mathscr{M}
is an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle over a groupoid equivalence, and as explained in Lemma
2.2
,

\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
and
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
are actions in the sense of
[
4
, Definition 2.10]
. Since they are assumed to commute, we therefore get Condition (FE1). For (FE2), note that the inner products are assumed to be sesquilinear in the correct sense. Furthermore,

(FE2.a)

is satisfied by
(DE2)
;

(FE2.b)

is satisfied by
(DE5)
;

(FE2.c)

is satisfied by
(DE4)
; and

(FE2.d)

is exactly Assumption
(3)
.

Lastly, for (FE3), we must check that each
M(x)
is an imprimitivity bimodule between
A(\rho(x))
and
B(\sigma(x))
. We know by
(DE9)
that it is a full left- and right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module. By Assumption
(3)
, the left- and right-inner products are compatible and the left-action is by
B(\sigma(x))
-adjointable operators; it remains to show that the right-action is by
A(\rho(x))
-adjointable operators. For
n_{i}\in M(x)

n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-59.10904pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 57.3646pt}}_{{\kern-60.10071pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 57.3646pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-50.26869pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 48.99092pt}}_{{\kern-51.72704pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 48.99092pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-47.67609pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 46.39832pt}}_{{\kern-49.13443pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 46.39832pt}}}}
, so
b
is an
A(\rho(x))
-adjointable operator.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

In the setting of Proposition
2.4
, it follows from
[
4
, Corollary 4.6.]
that Condition
(3)
actually also holds more generally: if
(a,m_{1},m_{2})\in A{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
, then
{\mathchoice{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty a^{*}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty a^{*}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty a^{*}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty a^{*}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
as elements of
B(h)
where
h=\mathchoice{\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1})^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty p_{\mathscr{A}}(a)^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1})^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty p_{\mathscr{A}}(a)^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1})^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty p_{\mathscr{A}}(a)^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{1})^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty p_{\mathscr{A}}(a)^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}q_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{2})\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}
. In other words,
\mathscr{A}
is acting on
\mathscr{M}
by ‘
\mathscr{B}
-adjointable’ operators. This observation foreshadows a connection to
[
1
]
, where results similar to the main theorem of the paper at hand have appeared. We will do a more in-depth comparison later in Corollary
6.7
, but let us already point out some points of distinction:
On the one hand, their result is more general in that they consider Fell bundles that are not necessarily separable or saturated;
instead of
(DE8)
, they only assume that
\mathscr{M}=(M\to X)
satisfies

(7R)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{span}}\{{\mathchoice{\langle M(x)\penalty\mid\penalty M(x)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle M(x)\penalty\mid\penalty M(x)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle M(x)\penalty\mid\penalty M(x)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle M(x)\penalty\mid\penalty M(x)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}:x\in Xu\}

\displaystyle=B(u)

On the other hand, their result is more restrictive in that our topological groupoid
\mathcal{H}
is replaced by a topological
group
. Moreover, they only consider
X=\mathcal{H}
, which is restrictive even in the case of groups (see Example
6.9
).

We have introduced all ingredients for the main theorem which says that any
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence can be rigged (in a unique way) to give a Fell bundle equivalence in the sense of
[
22
, Definition 6.1]
.

3.
The imprimitivity Fell bundle: Existence

\blacktriangleright

For the remainder of the paper
, we fix a Fell bundle
\mathscr{B}=(p_{\mathscr{B}}\colon B\to\mathcal{H})
over the groupoid
\mathcal{H}
and a right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence
\mathscr{M}=(q_{\mathscr{M}}\colon M\to X)
over the principal right
\mathcal{H}
-space
X
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{C}
are, like
\mathscr{B}
, Fell bundles over locally compact Hausdorff groupoids.
In
[
4
, Sections 5 and 6]
, it was shown that a so-called
hypo-equivalence

\mathscr{N}_{1}
from
\mathscr{A}
to
\mathscr{B}
and a hypo-equivalence
\mathscr{N}_{2}
from
\mathscr{B}
to
\mathscr{C}
can be ‘multiplied’ to yield a hypo-equivalence
\mathscr{N}_{1}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{N}_{2}
from
\mathscr{A}
to
\mathscr{C}
.
A careful examination of the proofs shows that not all the structure of hypo-equivalences is needed to construct the upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{N}_{1}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{N}_{2}
.
We will start this section by making this claim more precise.

Given
x,y\in X
with
u\coloneqq\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)
, the demi-equivalence
\mathscr{M}
gives us two full Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules over the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
B(u)
, namely the right-module
M(x)
and the
left
-module
M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
. In particular, combining the well-known results mentioned earlier, we get an imprimitivity bimodule between
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(x))
and
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(y)^{\mathrm{op}})
by taking the balanced tensor product:

(3.1)

\displaystyle K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\compacts_{B(u)}\left(M(y),M(x)\right),

where we write
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\otimes_{u}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
as short-hand for
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\otimes_{B(u)}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
.
The norm of this Banach space is, on sums of elementary tensors, given by

(3.2)

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i}m_{i}\otimes n_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\left\lvert n_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle m_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(x)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle m_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(x)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle m_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(x)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle m_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(x)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}}\right\rangle\left\langle n_{j}\right\rvert\right\|^{1/2}

operator norm on
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(y))

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}_{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}_{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-12.28586pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 12.67474pt}}_{{\kern-12.28586pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 12.67474pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.05664pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.44553pt}}_{{\kern-11.05664pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.44553pt}}}\left\langle m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(y)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(y)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(y)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle M(y)}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}}\right\rvert\right\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\text{operator norm on $\compacts_{B(u)}(M(x))$}.

We construct an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle over
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
as follows.

Lemma 3.1

(cf.
[
4
, Lemma 5.2]
)
.

On the set

K=\bigsqcup_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\in X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.03862pt\kern 5.31639pt}}_{{\kern-6.96715pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.31639pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.03862pt\kern 5.31639pt}}_{{\kern-6.96715pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.31639pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.10803pt\kern 3.3858pt}}_{{\kern-5.03656pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.3858pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-2.60803pt\kern 2.8858pt}}_{{\kern-4.53656pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 2.8858pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}}K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}}),

consider all cross-sections of the form

\mu\otimes\nu^{\mathrm{op}}\colon\quad X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to K,\qquad(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto\mu(x)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}},

for
\mu,\nu\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})
. Then there is a unique topology on

K
making it an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle over
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}

such that all cross-sections
\mu\otimes\nu^{\mathrm{op}}
are continuous.

In the literature
[
4
,
20
]
, the above bundle is denoted
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
. But since we fixed
\mathscr{M}
, we will ease notation and instead denote the bundle by
\mathscr{K}=(q_{\mathscr{K}}\colon K\to X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})
.

Proof.

Consider

\Gamma\coloneqq\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\mu\otimes\nu^{\mathrm{op}}:\mu,\nu\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})\}.

Recall that
\mathscr{M}
has enough continuous sections, so that for each
m\in M(x)
, there exists
\mu\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})
such that
\mu(x)=m
. In particular, it follows that

\{\gamma(x,y^{\mathrm{op}}):\gamma\in\Gamma\}
is dense in
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
.
If we can show for an arbitrary element
\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{i}\otimes\nu^{\mathrm{op}}_{i}
of
\Gamma
that
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto\left\|\gamma(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|
is upper semi-continuous, then the claim follows from

[
12
, Corollary 3.7]
(see also
[
FellDoranVol1
, 13.18]
,
[
29
, Theorem C.25]
,
[
4
, Remark 2.7]
).
We have by Equation (
3.2
)

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\left\lvert\nu_{i}(y)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\mu_{j}(x)\rangle\right\rangle\left\langle\nu_{j}(y)\right\rvert\right\|.

It was shown in
[
RaWi:Morita
, Lemma 2.65]
that the
k\times k
-matrix with
i,j
-entry
\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\mu_{j}(x)\rangle
is positive for each
x\in X
, so there exists a matrix
(b_{i,j}(x))_{i,j}
over
B(\sigma(x))
such that
\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\mu_{j}(x)\rangle=\sum\nolimits_{l=1}^{k}b_{il}(x)b_{jl}(x)^{*}
. Note that we may choose each
b_{i,j}\colon x\mapsto b_{i,j}(x)
to be a
continuous
section of the pullback bundle
\sigma^{*}(\mathscr{B})
.
For
1\leq l\leq k
, we let
\nu_{l}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{i}(y)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{il}(x)
. By continuity of
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}
, of each
\mu_{i}
and
b_{il}
, and of summation in
\mathscr{M}
, each
\nu_{l}
is continuous. Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum\nolimits_{l=1}^{k}\left\lvert\nu_{l}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\right\rangle\left\langle\nu_{l}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\right\rvert\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum\nolimits_{l=1}^{k}\langle\nu_{l}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{l}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma~\ref{lem:norm of compacts}}.

Since the
\mathscr{B}
-valued inner product on
\mathscr{M}
, summation in
\mathscr{B}
, and each
\nu_{l}
are continuous, and the norm on
\mathscr{B}
is upper semi-continuous, we conclude that
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto\left\|\gamma(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|
is upper semi-continuous.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

The proof of
[
4
, Lemma 5.4]
shows that, if we have two convergent nets
m_{\lambda}\to m
and
n_{\lambda}\to n
in
M
with
\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m_{\lambda})=\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(n_{\lambda})
, then
m_{\lambda}\otimes n_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}}\to m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}
in
K
.

Implicitly, Lemma
3.1
is making use of the ‘dual’ bundle to the demi-equivalence
\mathscr{B}

\mathscr{M}
: given a fibre
M(x)
of
\mathscr{M}
, its conjugate vector space
M(x)^{\mathrm{op}}
is, by definition, the fibre of

\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}=(M^{\mathrm{op}}\to X^{\mathrm{op}})
over
x^{\mathrm{op}}
. Clearly,
\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
is also an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle if
M^{\mathrm{op}}
and
X^{\mathrm{op}}
are given the same topologies as
M
and
X
, respectively. Moreover, the right
\mathscr{B}
-action on
\mathscr{M}
induces a left
\mathscr{B}
-action on
\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
if we define
b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m^{\mathrm{op}}\coloneqq(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}
. The reader should compare all of this with
[
22
, Example 6.7]
in the setting where
\mathscr{M}
is an equivalence.

Note that
\mathscr{K}
is the bundle-analogue of the space
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
in the world of groupoids and of the unbalanced tensor product
\mathbf{X}\otimes\mathbf{X^{\mathrm{op}}}
of Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules in the world of
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras. What we are actually after, though, is a bundle-analogue of their
quotients
, that is,
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
respectively
\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{X^{\mathrm{op}}}
:
To get
\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{X^{\mathrm{op}}}
from
\mathbf{X}\otimes\mathbf{X^{\mathrm{op}}}
, we quotient out by elements of the form

(\mathbf{x}\cdot a)\otimes\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\otimes(a\cdot\mathbf{y})

where
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{X^{\mathrm{op}}}
, and
a\in A
.
For bundles, such a difference does not make sense, since the two elementary tensors may live in different fibres: if
m\in M(x)
,
n\in M(y)
, and
b\in B(h)
are such that
\sigma(x)=r_{\mathcal{H}}(h)
and
s_{\mathcal{H}}(h)=\sigma(y)
, then

\displaystyle(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\in M(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)\otimes_{s(h)}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}},

while

\displaystyle m\otimes(b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}n^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\in M(x)\otimes_{r(h)}M(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}.

In order to identify
(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}
with
m\otimes(b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}n^{\mathrm{op}})=m\otimes(n\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}
, we therefore need a way to identify the above two fibres of
\mathscr{K}
:

Lemma 3.3

(cf.
[
4
, Theorem 5.20]
)
.

For any
u,v\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
and
h\in u\mathcal{H}v
, there exists a map

\Psi_{h}\colon\quad\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Xu\\
y\in Xv\end{subarray}}M(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)\otimes_{v}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Xu\\
y\in Xv\end{subarray}}M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}

determined on elementary tensors by

(3.3)

\Psi_{h}((m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}})=m\otimes(n\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*})^{\mathrm{op}},

where
\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(m)=u
,
\sigma_{\mathscr{M}}(n)=v
, and

b\in B(h)
. These maps have the following properties:

(
\Psi
1)

When restricted to a single fibre
K(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,y^{\mathrm{op}})\to K(x,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}})
, each
\Psi_{h}
is linear.

(
\Psi
2)

Each
\Psi_{h}
is isometric, i.e.,
\left\|\Psi_{h}(\xi)\right\|=\left\|\xi\right\|
.

(
\Psi
3)

\Psi_{h^{\prime}}\circ\Psi_{h}=\Psi_{h^{\prime}h}
for
(h^{\prime},h)\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}
.

(
\Psi
4)

\Psi_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}
is inverse to
\Psi_{h}
.

(
\Psi
5)

\Psi_{u}
for
u\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
is the identity map.

Proof.

We construct
\Psi_{h}
on each of the fibres
M(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)\otimes_{v}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Consider the map

M(x)\times B(h)\times M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\to M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}},\quad(m,b,n^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto m\otimes(n\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}.

Since it is multilinear, it descends to a linear map with domain
[M(x)\odot B(h)]\odot M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
, where
\odot
denotes the algebraic tensor product.
Because of the
B(u)
-balancing in the codomain, the map descends to a map with domain
[M(x)\odot_{B(u)}B(h)]\odot M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Since

[n\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{v}^{*}]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{*}=n\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}[bb_{v}]^{*}

for any
b_{v}\in B(v)
, it further descends to a map with domain
[M(x)\odot_{B(u)}B(h)]\odot_{B(v)}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
. It is easy to check that that map is isometric (when
M(x)\odot_{B(u)}B(h)
is given the norm of the balanced tensor product of Hilbert modules), and therefore extends to
[M(x)\otimes_{u}B(h)]\otimes_{v}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
. Now, Assumption
(DE8)
implies that every element of
M(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)
is the limit of sums of elements of the form
m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b
; in other words, the linear map
M(x)\odot_{B(u)}B(h)\to M(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)
determined by
m\odot b\mapsto m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b
has dense range.
We conclude the existence of
\Psi_{h}
. The remaining properties are easy to check.
∎

The collection of maps
\Psi_{h}
stitch together to give an isomorphism of upper semi-continuous Banach bundles as follows. Let
t\colon X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
be given by
t(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq\sigma(x)=\sigma^{\mathrm{op}}(y^{\mathrm{op}})
, and consider the continuous projection map

f\colon\quad\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})\to X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}},\quad(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto(x,y^{\mathrm{op}}).

The pull-back bundle of the upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{K}
via
f

is the bundle over the domain of
f

defined by

\displaystyle\bigl\{(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}},\xi)\in[\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})]\times K:f(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}})=q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\cong\bigl\{(h,\xi)\in\mathcal{H}\times K:s_{\mathcal{H}}(h)=t(q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi))\bigr\}.

While this bundle is often denoted by
f^{*}(\mathscr{K})
, we will denote it by
\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
instead, so that the letter
f
does not have to be introduced. We make an analogous definition for
\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}
.

It was shown in
[
4
, Lemma 2.8.]
that basic open sets of this upper semi-continuous Banach bundle are of the form
U{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}V
for
U\subseteq\mathcal{H}
,
V\subseteq K
open, and the sections

\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})\to\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K},\quad(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto(h,\tau(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})),

for
\tau
a continuous section of
\mathscr{K}
,
are the continuous sections that uniquely determine the topology on
\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
.

Lemma 3.4

(cf.
[
4
, Theorem 5.20.]
)
.

The map
