\displaystyle\Psi\colon\quad\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H},

\displaystyle(h,\xi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\Psi_{h}(\xi),h),

is an isomorphism of upper semi-continuous Banach bundles covering the homeomorphism

\displaystyle\psi\colon\quad\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\to(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}){\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H},

\displaystyle(h,x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,y^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(x,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}},h),

In particular,
\Psi
is jointly continuous.

We remind the reader that ‘
\Psi
covers
\psi
’ means that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}){\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

So given
\xi\in K
and
h\in\mathcal{H}
with
s_{\mathcal{H}}(h)=t(q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi))
, this means that

(3.4)

q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,y^{\mathrm{op}})\iff q_{\mathscr{K}}(\Psi_{h}(\xi))=(x,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}).

Proof.

To see that
\Psi
is an isomorphism, we will invoke Lemma
B.2
and
[
4
, Proposition A.8]
. Because of Lemma
3.1
and our comment preceding Lemma
3.4
, the set

\Gamma=\{(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto(h,\mu(x)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}}):\mu,\nu\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})\}

is a collection of continuous sections of
\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
. As the fibre of
\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
over a given
(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
is just
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and since
the linear span of
\{\mu(x)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}}:\mu,\nu\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})\}
is dense in
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
, we conclude that the linear span of
\{\gamma(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}}):\gamma\in\Gamma\}
is likewise dense in the fibre of
\mathcal{H}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
.
We claim that, given any element
\gamma\colon(h,x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto(h,\mu(x)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}})
of
\Gamma
,
the section

\Psi\circ\gamma\circ\psi^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}

of
\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}

is continuous. Indeed, it suffices to check that, if

(x_{\lambda},(y_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}},h_{\lambda})\to(x,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)^{\mathrm{op}},h)\text{ in }(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}){\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H},

then

\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}\bigl(\mu(x_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)\to\Psi_{h}\bigl(\mu(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)\text{ in }K{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.29321pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-5.64598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle t}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-4.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle t}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}.

Fix
\epsilon>0
. As mentioned earlier, Assumption
(DE8)
implies that
M(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)=M(x)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}B(h)
, so we may take finitely many elements
m_{i}\in M(x)
and
b_{i}\in B(h)
such that

(3.5)

\displaystyle\left\|\mu(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)-\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{i}\right\|<\epsilon.

For each
1\leq i\leq k
, fix a section
\mu_{i}
of
\mathscr{M}
and
\tau_{i}
of
\mathscr{B}
with
\mu_{i}(x)=m_{i}
respectively
\tau_{i}(h)=b_{i}
. Since addition in
\mathscr{M}
and the right
\mathscr{B}
-action are continuous,the net
\zeta_{\lambda}\coloneqq\sum_{i}\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\tau_{i}(h_{\lambda})
converges to
\zeta\coloneqq\sum_{i}\mu_{i}(x)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\tau_{i}(h)
in
M
.
By
[
4
, Lemma A.3,
(i)\implies(iii)
]
, we therefore have

(3.6)

\displaystyle\uplim_{\lambda}\left\|\mu(x_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})-\zeta_{\lambda}\right\|\leq\left\|\mu(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)-\zeta\right\|<\epsilon.

Note that

\displaystyle\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}\bigl(\zeta_{\lambda}\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\otimes\bigl(\nu(y_{\lambda})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\tau_{i}(h_{\lambda})^{*}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}},

and likewise without subscript-
\lambda
’s. Thus, by continuity of
\mu_{i},\nu,\tau_{i}
, of the involution in
\mathscr{B}
, of the right
\mathscr{B}
-action, and of addition in
\mathscr{K}
, combined with Remark
3.2
, implies that

(3.7)

\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\zeta_{\lambda}\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}})\to\Psi_{h}(\zeta\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}}).

Since
\Psi_{h}
is isometric
(

Ψ

2)
, we then have for large enough
\lambda

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\zeta_{\lambda}\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}})-\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\mu(x_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|=\left\|(\zeta_{\lambda}-\mu(x_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda}))\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\zeta_{\lambda}-\mu(x_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})\right\|\left\|\nu(y_{\lambda})\right\|\overset{(*)}{\leq}\epsilon(\epsilon+\left\|\nu(y)\right\|),

where
(*)
follows from (
3.6
) and continuity of
\nu
. The same computation without subscripts yields

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi_{h}(\zeta\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}})-\Psi_{h}(\mu(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|\leq\epsilon(\epsilon+\left\|\nu(y)\right\|).

As
\epsilon
was arbitrary, Lemma
B.1
states that this combined with (
3.7
) implies

\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}\bigl(\mu(x_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})\otimes\nu(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)\to\Psi_{h}\bigl(\mu(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr).

Because each
\Psi_{h}
is linear, isometric, and surjective,
the claim now follows from Lemma
B.2
and
[
4
, Proposition A.8]
.
∎

We are now able to proceed with the balancing by defining a relation
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
on the set
K
as follows; the results are adapted from
[
4
, Section 6]
.

Lemma 3.5
.

If
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in K
with
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{i})=(x_{i},y_{i}^{\mathrm{op}})
, let

\xi_{1}\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}\xi_{2}\qquad:\iff\qquad\exists h\in\sigma(x_{1})\mathcal{H}\sigma(x_{2})\text{ such that }\quad x_{2}=x_{1}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,\quad y_{2}=y_{1}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,\quad\Psi_{h}(\xi_{2})=\xi_{1}.

This defines a closed equivalence relation on the total space
K
of
\mathscr{K}
whose quotient map
Q\colon K\to K/\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
is open.

We often write
[\xi]
for
Q(\xi)
.

Proof.

The proof that
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
is closed is verbatim that given for
[
4
, Lemma 6.2]
, where it is also explained why
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
is an equivalence relation.

To show that the quotient map
Q
is open, the proof of
[
4
, Proposition 6.8]
goes through for
\mathscr{N}\coloneqq\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
, even though
\mathscr{M}
was assumed to be an equivalence and
\mathcal{H}
was assumed to be étale in
[
4
]
.
In fact, étaleness was only needed to be allowed to invoke
[
4
, Lemma 6.10]
, but that lemma’s proof holds as long as the range map of
\mathcal{H}
is open (which we assumed here).
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

On the quotient of
K
by
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
, the map
p\colon K/\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}\to\mathcal{G},[\xi]\mapsto[q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)]
, is well defined, surjective, continuous, and open.

Proof.

Verbatim the proof of
[
4
, Lemma 6.5]
for
\mathscr{N}\coloneqq\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
, even though
\mathscr{M}
was assumed to be an equivalence in that lemma.
∎

\blacktriangleright

For the moment
, we will denote the quotient bundle of
\mathscr{K}
by
\mathscr{A}=(p_{\mathscr{A}}=p\colon A\to\mathcal{G})
. Once we have shown that
\mathscr{A}
is a Fell bundle that is equivalent to
\mathscr{B}
via
\mathscr{M}
, we will change notation.

Lemma 3.7

(cf.
[
4
, Proposition 6.6. and Lemma 6.7.]
)
.

Fix
g=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}\in\mathcal{G}
. With respect to the subspace topology of
A=K/\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
on the fibre
A(g)
of
\mathscr{A}
, the restriction of the quotient map
Q\colon K\to A
to the fibre over
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
is a homeomorphism:

(3.8)

\displaystyle Q_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}\colon\quad K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})=M(x)\otimes_{\sigma(x)}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\approx}{\longrightarrow}A(g).

The above lemma means that we can give
A(g)
the Banach space structure that makes all maps
Q_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}

isomorphisms
: for
[\xi_{1}],[\xi_{2}]\in A(g)
,
there exists a unique
h\in\mathcal{H}
such that
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{1})=q_{\mathscr{K}}(\Psi_{h}(\xi_{2}))
, and we may let

\lambda_{1}[\xi_{1}]+\lambda_{2}[\xi_{2}]\coloneqq[\lambda_{1}\xi_{1}+\lambda_{2}\Psi_{h}(\xi_{2})]\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|[\xi]\right\|\coloneqq\left\|\xi\right\|_{K(q(\xi))}.

Note that, in light of the Hilbert module isomorphism in Equation (
3.1
), we can also think of the fibres of
\mathscr{A}
as generalized compact operators:

(3.9)

\displaystyle\compacts_{B(\sigma(x))}\bigl(M(y),M(x)\bigr)\overset{\approx}{\longrightarrow}A([x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]).

Proof.

If
\xi,\eta\in K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
, then since the
\mathcal{H}
-action on
X
is free,
h=\sigma(x)\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
is the only possible element that allows
\xi\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}\eta
, in which case
\Psi_{h}(\eta)=\eta
(since
h
is a unit) and hence
\xi=\eta
. Thus,
Q_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
is injective. For surjectivity, note that
Q
is surjective, so any element
a
of
A(g)
can be written as
a=Q(\xi)
for
\xi\in K
with
[q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)]=g=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
. In particular, there exists
h\in\mathcal{H}
with
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)^{\mathrm{op}})
, so that
\Psi_{h}(\xi)\in K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and
a=Q(\xi)=Q_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}(\Psi_{h}(\xi))
.

Since
p=p_{\mathscr{A}}
is continuous,
A(g)=p_{\mathscr{A}}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(\{g\})
is closed in
A
, and so continuity of
Q
implies continuity of
Q_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
. To see that
Q_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
is closed (and hence a homeomorphism), we follow the idea in the proof of
[
4
, Lemma 6.7.]
: Let
F\subseteq K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
be a closed set. To show that
Q(F)\subseteq A(g)
is closed, it suffices to show that it is closed in
A
, i.e., that
Q^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(Q(F))
is closed in
K
. So assume
\xi_{\lambda}\to\xi
in
K
with
Q(\xi_{\lambda})\in Q(F)
; we must prove that
Q(\xi)\in Q(F)
. Since
Q(\xi_{\lambda})\in F
, there exist
\eta_{\lambda}\in F
with
\eta_{\lambda}\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}\xi_{\lambda}
. Since
F\subseteq K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
, this means there exist
h_{\lambda}\in\sigma(x)\mathcal{H}
with
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{\lambda})=(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda},(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}})
and
\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\xi_{\lambda})=\eta_{\lambda}
. Since
\xi_{\lambda}\to\xi
, we have

(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda},(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}})=q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{\lambda})\to q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=:(x_{0},y_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}).

Since the
\mathcal{H}
-action on
X
is proper, it follows from
[
28
, Proposition 2.17]
that (a subnet of)
\{h_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
converges to, say,
h
; since
X
is Hausdorff, it follows that
(x_{0},y_{0}^{\mathrm{op}})=(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)^{\mathrm{op}})
. Since
\Psi
is jointly continuous, we conclude that (a subnet of)
\eta_{\lambda}=\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\xi_{\lambda})
converges to
\Psi_{h}(\xi)

in
K
. Since
\eta_{\lambda}\in F
and
F
is closed in
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and hence also in
K
, we have
\Psi_{h}(\xi)\in F
. Since
\xi\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}\Psi_{h}(\xi)
, this means that
\xi\in Q^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(Q(F))
, as needed.
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

The bundle
\mathscr{A}
- equipped with the quotient topology on
A=K/\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
and with the fibrewise linear structure inherited from
\mathscr{K}
via the maps given in (
3.8
) - is an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle. With respect to this topology, all sections of the form

[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto[\mu(x)\otimes\nu(y)^{\mathrm{op}}]

are continuous, where
\mu,\nu
are continuous sections of
\mathscr{M}
.

Proof.

That
\mathscr{A}
is an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle follows from an application of
[
4
, Proposition 6.13]
; here, we need that both
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-26.39096pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 23.10555pt}}_{{\kern-25.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 23.10555pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-26.39096pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 23.10555pt}}_{{\kern-25.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 23.10555pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-21.23428pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 17.94887pt}}_{{\kern-20.8065pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 17.94887pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-18.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 15.67776pt}}_{{\kern-18.5354pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 15.67776pt}}}\colon X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{G}
and
Q\colon K\to A
are open quotient maps (Lemma
3.5
) and that

is surjective (Lemma
3.7
) and a linear isometry (by definition of the linear structure on each fibre of
\mathscr{A}
).

That the given section is continuous follows since
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-21.23428pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 17.94887pt}}_{{\kern-20.8065pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 17.94887pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\right\}^{{\kern-18.96318pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 15.67776pt}}_{{\kern-18.5354pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 15.67776pt}}}
is open,

is a continuous section of
\mathscr{K}
(Lemma
3.1
), and
Q
is continuous.
∎

At this point, we will diverge from what was done in
[
4
]
and prove that

\mathscr{A}=(p_{\mathscr{A}}\colon A\to\mathcal{G})

is not just an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle but actually a Fell bundle; see Theorem
3.18
. In particular, we need to construct two maps, namely a multiplication
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\cdot\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\colon\mathscr{A}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}\to A
and an involution
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}^{*}\colon A\to A
. Conceptually, the involution is easier, so this is where we will start.

Lemma 3.9
.

There exists a unique bijective, fibrewise isometric and conjugate-linear map
\mathtt{Flip}\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{K}
that covers the homeomorphism

\mathtt{flip}\colon X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}},\quad(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto(y,x^{\mathrm{op}}),

and that is fibrewise given on dense spanning elements by

(3.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})&\to K(y,x^{\mathrm{op}})\\
m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}&\mapsto n\otimes m^{\mathrm{op}}.\end{split}

Note that the following diagram commutes:

(3.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(y,(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(x,(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}},x^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathtt{Flip}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathtt{Flip}}

Proof.

To see that the map
\mathtt{Flip}
exists on each fibre, fix
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\in X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
and let
u=\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)
. Recall that
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
is isomorphic to
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(y),M(x))
as bi-Hilbert
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(x))-\compacts_{B(u)}(M(y))
-bimodules. With this identification, the restriction of
\mathtt{Flip}
to this fibre is given by

\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}_{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}_{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}}\left\langle n\right\rvert\mapsto\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert n\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.22113pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.61002pt}}_{{\kern-11.22113pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.61002pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert n\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.22113pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.61002pt}}_{{\kern-11.22113pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.61002pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert n\right\rangle^{{\kern-9.21895pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.60783pt}}_{{\kern-9.21895pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.60783pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert n\right\rangle^{{\kern-8.37862pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 8.7675pt}}_{{\kern-8.37862pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 8.7675pt}}}\left\langle m\right\rvert,

or in other words,
\mathtt{Flip}
is the adjoint map
T\mapsto T^{*}
. In particular,
\mathtt{Flip}
is fibrewise antilinear and isometric. Its continuity is built into the topology on
\mathscr{K}
:
If
\mu,\nu
are continuous sections of
\mathscr{M}
, then
\mu\otimes\nu^{\mathrm{op}}
and
\nu\otimes\mu^{\mathrm{op}}
are continuous sections of
\mathscr{K}
. Since
\mathtt{Flip}
transforms the former into the latter, it is continuous and open by an application of
[
4
, Propositions A.7 and A.8]
.
5
5

5

To be pedantic,
[
4
]
only deals with fibrewise linear rather than antilinear maps, so the cited results give an isomorphism
\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}
of Banach bundles determined by
m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}\mapsto(n\otimes m^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}
covering the map
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto(y,x^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}
. But we can then compose that map with the homeomorphism
\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{K}
,
\xi^{\mathrm{op}}\mapsto\xi
.

∎

Continuity of
\mathtt{Flip}
and continuity and openness of the quotient map
Q
now immediately imply the following:

Corollary 3.10
.

On the quotient bundle
\mathscr{A}
, there exists a unique continuous, fibrewise conjugate-linear map
\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}^{\ast}\colon A\to A
given by
[\xi]^{*}=[\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)]
.

Now that we have (a candidate for) the involution on
\mathscr{A}
, we proceed to construct the multiplication. Recall that our end goal is not only to show that
\mathscr{A}
is a Fell bundle but also that there is a left action
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}
of
\mathscr{A}
on
\mathscr{M}
. We want this action to behave nicely with respect to the multiplication in the sense that
a_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(a_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m)=(a_{1}\cdot a_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m
; it is therefore easier to
first
construct a candidate for the left-action and then use it to construct the multiplication. Like with the involution, we first do everything on the level of
\mathscr{K}
before we move to its quotient
\mathscr{A}
. For clarity, we remind the reader that the fibre of
\mathscr{K}
over
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\in X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
is given by

K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})=M(x)\otimes_{\sigma(x)}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

For
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\in X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
, there exists a continuous bilinear map

\Phi_{x,y}\colon\quad K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\times M(y)\to M(x)

determined on elementary tensors by

(3.12)

\Phi_{x,y}(m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}},k)=m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}.

For all
\xi\in K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and
k\in M(y)
, it satisfies

(3.13)

\left\|\Phi_{x,y}(\xi,k)\right\|\leq\left\|\xi\right\|\left\|k\right\|.

Proof.

The existence of
\Phi_{x,y}
follows from the following, well-studied isomorphisms of bi-Hilbert
A-A
-modules for any full right-Hilbert
A
-module
\mathbf{Y}
: Firstly,
\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes_{\compacts}\mathbf{Y}\to A
determined by

\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes\mathbf{y}_{2}\mapsto{\mathchoice{\langle\mathbf{y}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{Y}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{y}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{Y}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{y}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{Y}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}{\langle\mathbf{y}_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\mathbf{y}_{1}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{Y}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}}}
, and secondly,
\mathbf{Y}\otimes_{A}A\to\mathbf{Y}
determined by
\mathbf{y}\otimes a\mapsto\mathbf{y}\cdot a
.
In our situation,
A=B(u)
where
u=\sigma(x)=\sigma(y)
, and we use first
M(y)
and then
M(x)
to take the rôle of
\mathbf{Y}
. To be precise, if we write
\compacts
for
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(y))
, then

K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\times M(y)\twoheadrightarrow\bigl(M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)\otimes_{\compacts}M(y)\cong M(x)\otimes_{u}B(u)\cong M(x).

The claim about the norm is also well known, but we add it here for completion. Since
\Phi_{x,y}
is continuous, it suffices to prove the claim for
\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}m_{i}\otimes n_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}
a sum of elementary tensors.
We have

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi_{x,y}(\xi,k)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i}m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|^{2}=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle 143.81499pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\rvert\right\|,

where the norm on the right-hand side is the operator norm on
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(x))
. Note that
{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\in B(u)
acts by
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(x))
-adjointable operators, so that

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi_{x,y}(\xi,k)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}_{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\right\rvert\right\|.

Now,
{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
by
(DE4)
. Since
k,n_{i}\in M(y)
, we further know that
k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle k\penalty\mid\penalty n_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}_{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}_{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-8.983pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.37189pt}}_{{\kern-8.983pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.37189pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-8.21008pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 8.59897pt}}_{{\kern-8.21008pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 8.59897pt}}}\left\langle k\right\rvert(n_{i})
. Recall that
\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}_{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}_{{\kern-10.69797pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.08685pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-8.983pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.37189pt}}_{{\kern-8.983pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.37189pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert k\right\rangle^{{\kern-8.21008pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 8.59897pt}}_{{\kern-8.21008pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 8.59897pt}}}\left\langle k\right\rvert
is a positive element of
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(y))
, so we can write it as
T^{*}T
. In particular,

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi_{x,y}(\xi,k)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}_{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}_{{\kern-15.56203pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 15.95091pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-12.28586pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 12.67474pt}}_{{\kern-12.28586pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 12.67474pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\right\rangle^{{\kern-11.05664pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.44553pt}}_{{\kern-11.05664pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.44553pt}}}\left\langle m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{{\mathchoice{\langle Tn_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty Tn_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle Tn_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty Tn_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle Tn_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty Tn_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle Tn_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty Tn_{i}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}}\right\rvert\right\|\overset{\eqref{eq:norm on compacts}}{=}\left\|\sum_{i}m_{i}\otimes(Tn_{i})^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|^{2}

Since
\left\|\sum_{i}m_{i}\otimes(Tn_{i})^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|\leq\left\|1\otimes T\right\|\left\|\sum_{i}m_{i}\otimes n_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|
and
\left\|1\otimes T\right\|=\left\|T\right\|=\left\|k\right\|
, we conclude that

\left\|\Phi_{x,y}(\xi,k)\right\|\leq\left\|\xi\right\|\left\|k\right\|,

as claimed.
∎

Bilinearity of
\Phi_{x,y}
helps us prove the following result.

Lemma 3.12
.

For any
u\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
and any
y\in Xu
, there exists a map

U_{y}\colon\quad\bigsqcup_{x,z\in Xu}K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\times K(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})\to\bigsqcup_{x,z\in Xu}K(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})

determined by

(3.14)

U_{y}(m\otimes n_{1}^{\mathrm{op}},n_{2}\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}})=(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}}.

These maps have the following properties:

(U1)

When restricted to a fibre
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\times K(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})\to K(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})
,
U_{y}
is bilinear.

(U2)

Each
U_{y}
satisfies
\left\|U_{y}(\xi,\eta)\right\|\leq\left\|\xi\right\|\left\|\eta\right\|

and
\left\|U_{y}\bigl(\xi,\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)\bigr)\right\|=\left\|\xi\right\|^{2}
.

(U3)

If
h\in u\mathcal{H}
, then

\Psi_{h}\circ U_{y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h}=U_{y}\circ(\Psi_{h}\times\Psi_{h})
.

(U4)

We have

\mathtt{Flip}\bigl(U_{y}(\xi,\eta)\bigr)=U_{y}\Bigl(\mathtt{Flip}(\eta),\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)\Bigr)
.

Note that ‘equivariance’ of
U
(Condition
(U3)
)
is equivalent to commutativity of the following diagram for all
x,z
:

(3.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y^{\mathrm{op}})\times K(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}z^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}z^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\times K(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{h}\times\Psi_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{y}}

Proof of Lemma
3.12
.

The existence of
U_{y}
follows from observations of general Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules similar to those following Proposition
1.5
:
If
\mathbf{X,Y,Z}
are full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules over a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
, then the maps

(3.16)

are isomorphisms of bi-Hilbert
\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{X})-\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{Z})
-modules. The above isomorphism can be precomposed with the universal bilinear map
\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathbf{Y}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}\to(\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{op}})\otimes_{\compacts_{A}(\mathbf{Y})}(\mathbf{Y}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{op}})
. We apply this to the case where
\mathbf{Y}=M(y)
and
A=B(u)
. Condition
(U1)
is therefore by construction.

(U2)

Since the maps in (
3.16
) are isomorphisms of modules (in particular, they are isometric), we have

\left\|U_{y}(\xi,\eta)\right\|=\left\|\xi\otimes\eta\right\|
for
\xi\in K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and
\eta\in K(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})
, which is known (or easily shown) to be
bounded by
\left\|\xi\right\|\left\|\eta\right\|
. In particular,
U_{y}
is not just linear but also continuous on each fibre.
For the second claim, it therefore suffices to consider one of the dense elements
\xi=\sum_{i}m_{i}\otimes n_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}
in which case
\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)=\sum_{j}n_{j}\otimes m_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}
and

(3.17)

\displaystyle U(\xi,\mathtt{Flip}(\xi))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})\otimes m_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}.

Again, since the maps in (
3.16
) are isometric, we see that

\displaystyle\left\|U(\xi,\mathtt{Flip}(\xi))\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i,j}\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\rangle^{{\kern-156.71211pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 157.101pt}}_{{\kern-156.71211pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 157.101pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\rangle^{{\kern-156.71211pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 157.101pt}}_{{\kern-156.71211pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 157.101pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty which equals
\left\|\xi\right\|^{2}
by Equation (

).

(U3)
The properties of
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}

imply that

(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle For

,
suppose we are given elements

,
m\in M(x),n_{1}\in M(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h),n_{2}\in M(y),k\in M(z\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h)
. Then

\displaystyle(\Psi_{h}\circ U_{y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h})\bigl((m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1})\otimes n_{1},(n_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{2})\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{h}\bigl((m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty
