n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)=\Psi_{h}\bigl([m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(b_{1}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{2}\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1}^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1}^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1}^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1}^{*}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\bigr)\otimes(b_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}k^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle=U_{y}\bigl(m\otimes(b_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}n_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}),n_{2}\otimes(b_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}k^{\mathrm{op}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=(U_{y}\circ\Psi_{h}\times\Psi_{h})\bigl((m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{1})\otimes n_{1},(n_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{2})\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr).

Because of continuity and (bi)linearity of
\Psi_{h}
and of
U_{y},U_{y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h}
, we conclude that
\Psi_{h}\circ U_{y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h}=U_{y}\circ\Psi_{h}\times\Psi_{h}
, as claimed. One likewise checks that
(U4)
holds for for elementary tensors, and again uses (bi)linearity and continuity of
U_{y}
and of
\mathtt{Flip}
to deduce the claim.
∎

Similarly to how we constructed
\Psi
in Lemma
3.4
out of the maps
\Psi_{h}
from Lemma
3.3
, we now want to ‘stich together’ the maps
\Phi_{x,y}
on the one hand and the maps
U_{y}
on the other hand, to bundle maps. To do so, we first need a definition.

Definition 3.13
.

For two upper semi-continuous Banach bundles
\mathscr{M}=(M\to X)
and
\mathscr{N}=(N\to Y)
, consider the product bundle
\mathscr{M}\times\mathscr{N}=(M\times N\to X\times Y)
; the norm on its fibre over
(x,y)
can be chosen as the maximum of the norms of
M(x)
and
N(y)
, and it is given the component-wise vector space structure. The global topology of the total space
M\times N

of
\mathscr{M}\times\mathscr{N}
is induced by the
\mathbb{C}
-linear span of sections of the form

(x,y)\mapsto(\mu(x),\nu(y)),

where
\mu
and
\nu
are continuous sections of
\mathscr{M}
respectively
\mathscr{N}
.

If

f\colon X\to Z
and
g\colon Y\to Z
are continuous functions into some other topological space, then we write
\mathscr{M}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.11961pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.11961pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.47238pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.77238pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{N}
for the restriction of
\mathscr{M}\times\mathscr{N}
to the closed subset
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.11961pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.11961pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.47238pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle f}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.77238pt{\scriptscriptstyle f}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle g}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}Y
of the base
X\times Y
.

We now let

\mathtt{fir},\mathtt{sec}\colon X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to X
be given by

\mathtt{fir}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})=x
respectively

\mathtt{sec}(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})=y
(“
\mathtt{fir}
” for “first”, “
\mathtt{sec}
” for “second”).

Lemma 3.14
.

Writing
\Phi_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
for the map
\Phi_{x,y}
of Lemma
3.11
, the map

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\quad\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{M}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M},

\displaystyle(\xi,m)

\displaystyle\mapsto\Phi_{q(\xi)}(\xi,m),

is bilinear, jointly continuous, and covers the continuous surjection

\displaystyle(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}){\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X

\displaystyle\to X,

\displaystyle(x,y^{\mathrm{op}},y)

\displaystyle\mapsto x.

Note that
\Phi
is not a homomorphism of Banach bundles, since
\Phi
is not fibrewise linear.

Proof.

Since
\Phi_{x,y}
lands in
M(x)
by construction,
\Phi
covers
\mathtt{fir}\colon(x,y^{\mathrm{op}},y)\mapsto x
.
To see that
\Phi
is jointly continuous, assume that we are given a convergent net
(\xi_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})\to(\xi,k)
in
K{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
, and let
q(\xi_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})=(x_{\lambda},y_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}},y_{\lambda})
and
q(\xi,k)=(x,y^{\mathrm{op}},y)
; note that
x_{\lambda}\to x
and
y_{\lambda}\to y
in
X
. To prove that
\Phi(\xi_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})\to\Phi(\xi,k)
, fix
\delta>0
; we must find
\kappa\in\Gamma_{0}(X;\mathscr{M})
with
\left\|\Phi(\xi,k)-\kappa(x)\right\|<\delta
and
\left\|\Phi(\xi_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})-\kappa(x_{\lambda})\right\|<\delta
for large
\lambda

[
4
, Lemma A.3]
.

Because of how the topology on
\mathscr{K}
is defined and because
\xi_{\lambda}\to\xi
in
\mathscr{K}
, we can find
finitely many nets

m_{j,\lambda}\to m_{j}
and
n_{j,\lambda}\to n_{j}
in
M

such that

(3.18)

\left\|\xi-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\otimes n_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|<\frac{\delta}{2(\left\|k\right\|+1)},\quad\left\|\xi_{\lambda}-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j,\lambda}\otimes n_{j,\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|<\frac{\delta}{2(\left\|k\right\|+1)}.

Since
{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
is jointly continuous, this implies that for each
j
,
{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\to{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
in
B
. Since
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
is jointly continuous, this in turn implies that
\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j,\lambda}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\to\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
in
M
. That means that we can find a section
\kappa
of
\mathscr{M}
with

(3.19)

\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}-\kappa(x)\right\|<\frac{\delta}{2},\quad\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j,\lambda}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j,\lambda}\penalty\mid\penalty k_{\lambda}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}-\kappa(x_{\lambda})\right\|<\frac{\delta}{2}

for all
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
.
Let
\lambda
be large enough such that we also have
|\left\|k_{\lambda}\right\|-\left\|k\right\||<1.
Linearity of
\Phi
in the first component yields:

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi(\xi,k)-\kappa(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Phi(\xi,k)-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|+\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{j}\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}-\kappa(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:close via kappa}}{\leq}\left\|\Phi\bigl(\xi-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\otimes n_{j}^{\mathrm{op}},k\bigr)\right\|+\frac{\delta}{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:Phi:contractive}}{\leq}\left\|\xi-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}m_{j}\otimes n_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|\left\|k\right\|+\frac{\delta}{2}\overset{\eqref{eq:close via tau}}{<}\delta,

and the exact same computation with subscript-
\lambda
’s yields
\left\|\Phi(\xi_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})-\kappa(x_{\lambda})\right\|<\delta
for large
\lambda
, which proves the claim.
∎

We proceed with stitching together the
U_{y}
’s:

Lemma 3.15
.

The map

\displaystyle U\colon\quad\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{K},

\displaystyle(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle\mapsto U_{\mathtt{sec}(\xi)}(\xi,\eta),

is bilinear, jointly continuous, and covers the continuous surjection

\displaystyle(X\times X^{\mathrm{op}}){\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}(X\times X^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\to X\times X^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(x,y^{\mathrm{op}},y,z^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(x,z^{\mathrm{op}}).

Moreover, given
\mu,\xi,\eta\in K
and
m\in M
with
(\mu,\xi),(\xi,\eta)\in\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
and
(\xi,m)\in\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle q}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{M}
, we have

(3.20)

\displaystyle\Phi(U(\mu,\xi),m)

\displaystyle=\Phi(\mu,\Phi(\xi,m))

and thus

\displaystyle U\bigl(U(\mu,\xi),\eta\bigr)

\displaystyle=U\bigl(\mu,U(\xi,\eta)\bigr).

Proof.

To see that
U
is jointly continuous, assume that
\{(\xi_{\lambda},\eta_{\lambda})\}_{\lambda}
is a net in
\mathscr{K}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-11.54176pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-9.89453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-9.19453pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{sec}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fir}}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{K}
that converges to
(\xi,\eta)
. Let
(x_{\lambda},y_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{\lambda})
, which converges to
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)
, and let
(y_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq q_{\mathscr{K}}(\eta_{\lambda})
, which converges to
(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})\coloneqq q_{\mathscr{K}}(\eta)
.
Fix an arbitrary
\delta\in(0,1)
; by
[
4
, Lemma A.3]
, we should find a section
\kappa\in\Gamma_{0}(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}};\mathscr{K})
with

\left\|U\bigl(\xi,\eta\bigr)-\kappa(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|<4\delta\text{ and }\left\|U\bigl(\xi_{\lambda},\eta_{\lambda}\bigr)-\kappa(x_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|<4\delta

for large
\lambda
.
Because of how the topology on
\mathscr{K}
is defined and because
\xi_{\lambda}\to\xi
,
\eta_{\lambda}\to\eta
in
\mathscr{K}
,
there exist elements

\zeta_{\lambda}\in M(x_{\lambda})\odot M(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\text{ converging to }\zeta\in M(x)\odot M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\text{ in }K

and elements

\chi_{\lambda}\in M(y_{\lambda})\odot M(z_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\text{ converging to }\chi\in M(y)\odot M(z)^{\mathrm{op}}\text{ in }K

such that

(3.21)

\displaystyle\left\|\xi-\zeta\right\|,\left\|\xi_{\lambda}-\zeta_{\lambda}\right\|<\frac{\delta}{\left\|\eta\right\|+1},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\eta-\chi\right\|,\left\|\eta_{\lambda}-\chi_{\lambda}\right\|<\frac{\delta}{\left\|\xi\right\|+1}

for large
\lambda
. Here, it is important to point out that
\odot
refers to the
algebraic
tensor product, so each of the elements considered above is a finite sum of elementary tensors.
Note that

(3.22)

U(m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}},k\otimes\ell^{\mathrm{op}})=\Phi(m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}},k)\otimes\ell^{\mathrm{op}},

so using bilinearity of
U
and continuity of
\Phi
, the fact that
\zeta_{\lambda}\to\zeta
and
\chi_{\lambda}\to\chi
in
K
and that addition and scalar multiplication is continuous on
\mathscr{K}
, imply that

U(\zeta_{\lambda},\chi_{\lambda})\to U(\zeta,\chi)

for these particular sums of elementary tensors
\zeta_{\lambda},\chi_{\lambda}
.
This convergence means that we can find a section
\kappa
of
\mathscr{K}
with

(3.23)

\left\|U(\zeta_{\lambda},\chi_{\lambda})-\kappa(x_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|<\delta\text{ and }\left\|U(\zeta,\chi)-\kappa(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|<\delta.

Combining this with Condition
(U2)
, we conclude for large
\lambda
:

\displaystyle\left\|U\bigl(\xi,\eta\bigr)-\kappa(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|U\bigl(\xi,[\eta-\chi]\bigr)\right\|+\left\|U\bigl([\xi-\zeta],\chi\bigr)\right\|+\left\|U\bigl(\zeta,\chi\bigr)-\kappa(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|

\displaystyle<\left\|\xi\right\|\,\left\|\eta-\chi\right\|+\left\|\xi-\zeta\right\|\,\left\|\chi\right\|+\delta

by (
3.23
),
(U2)

\displaystyle<\delta+\frac{\delta}{\left\|\eta\right\|+1}\,\frac{\delta}{\left\|\xi\right\|+1}+\delta\leq 3\delta

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:all four close}}.

We likewise get for
\lambda
large enough such that
|\left\|\xi\right\|-\left\|\xi_{\lambda}\right\||
and
|\left\|\eta\right\|-\left\|\eta_{\lambda}\right\||
are bounded by
1
,

\displaystyle\left\|U\bigl(\xi_{\lambda},\eta_{\lambda}\bigr)-\kappa(x_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|

\displaystyle<\left\|\xi_{\lambda}\right\|\,\left\|\eta_{\lambda}-\chi_{\lambda}\right\|+\left\|\xi_{\lambda}-\zeta_{\lambda}\right\|\,\left\|\chi_{\lambda}\right\|+\delta

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:all four close}}{<}(\left\|\xi\right\|+1)\,\frac{\delta}{\left\|\xi\right\|+1}+\frac{\delta}{\left\|\eta\right\|+1}\,\left\|\chi_{\lambda}\right\|+\delta.

Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\chi_{\lambda}\right\|\leq\left\|\chi_{\lambda}-\eta_{\lambda}\right\|+\left\|\eta_{\lambda}\right\|\leq\left\|\chi_{\lambda}-\eta_{\lambda}\right\|+\left\|\eta\right\|+1\leq\frac{\delta}{\left\|\xi\right\|+1}+\left\|\eta\right\|+1

so that

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{\left\|\eta\right\|+1}\,\left\|\chi_{\lambda}\right\|\leq\frac{\delta}{\left\|\eta\right\|+1}\,\left[\frac{\delta}{\left\|\xi\right\|+1}+\left\|\eta\right\|+1\right]\leq 2\delta.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|U(\xi_{\lambda},\eta_{\lambda})-\kappa(x_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\right\|<(\left\|\xi\right\|+1)\,\frac{\delta}{\left\|\xi\right\|+1}+2\delta+\delta<4\delta,

as needed.

For (
3.20
), it likewise suffices to prove the claims for elementary tensors
\mu=\ell\otimes m_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
\xi=m_{2}\otimes n_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and
\eta=n_{2}\otimes k^{\mathrm{op}}
. The computations make use of
\mathscr{B}
-linearity of
{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
and are left to the reader.∎

For the next result, we remind the reader that we denote the groupoid
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}=(X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})/\mathcal{H}
by
\mathcal{G}
and that we write
r_{\mathscr{A}}\coloneqq r_{\mathcal{G}}\circ p_{\mathscr{A}}
and
s_{\mathscr{A}}\coloneqq s_{\mathcal{G}}\circ p_{\mathscr{A}}
.

Lemma 3.16
.

On the quotient bundle
\mathscr{A}=(p_{\mathscr{A}}\colon A\to\mathcal{G})
, the following map is well defined, continuous, and fibrewise bilinear:

\displaystyle\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\cdot\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\colon\qquad\mathscr{A}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}\coloneqq\left\{([\xi],[\eta])\in A\times A:s_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi])=r_{\mathscr{A}}([\eta])\right\}\to A,

given by

\displaystyle[\xi]\cdot[\eta]\coloneqq\Bigl[U\bigl(\xi,\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)\Bigr],

where
U
is as defined in Lemma
3.15
and
h\in\mathcal{H}
is the unique element such that
\mathtt{sec}_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=\mathtt{fir}_{\mathscr{K}}\bigl(\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)
.

Remark 3.17
.

If
(g_{1},g_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}
, we may choose representatives
(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and
(y^{\mathrm{op}},z)
of
g_{1}
respectively
g_{2}
. If we then identify the fibres
A(g_{1}),A(g_{2})
with
K(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})=M(x)\otimes_{\sigma(x)}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
and
K(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})
via (
3.8
), then the map
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\cdot\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
is fibrewise on dense spanning sets given by

(3.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\cdot\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\colon\qquad A(g_{1})\times A(g_{2})&\to K(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})\cong A(g_{1}g_{2})\\
\bigl(m_{1}\otimes n_{1}^{\mathrm{op}},m_{2}\otimes n_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)&\mapsto(m_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})\otimes n_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
3.16
.

Since
U_{h}\circ(\Psi_{h}\times\Psi_{h})=\Psi_{h}\circ U_{y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h}
, the map
\mathfrak{m}\coloneqq\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\cdot\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
is well defined. Since
U_{y}
and
\Psi_{h}
are linear, linearity of
\mathfrak{m}
follows directly from the definition of the fibrewise Banach space structure on
\mathscr{A}
.

To see that it is continuous, we will invoke openness of the quotient map
Q
: Suppose
\{\chi_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
is a net in
\mathscr{A}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}
which converges to
\chi
. Since it suffices to show that a
subnet
of
\{\mathfrak{m}(\chi_{\lambda})\}_{\lambda}
converges to
\mathfrak{m}(\chi)
, we can without loss of generality assume that the
entire
net
\{\chi_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
lifts to a net
\{(\xi_{\lambda},\eta_{\lambda})\}_{\lambda}
in
K\times K
that converges to a lift
(\xi,\eta)
of
\chi
(here, we have made use of
[
28
, Proposition 1.1 (Fell’s criterion)]
for the open map
Q
).
Denote
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{\lambda})=(x_{\lambda},y_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
Since
\chi_{\lambda}\in\mathscr{A}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}
, we may let
h_{\lambda},h\in\mathcal{H}
be the unique elements such that
q_{\mathscr{K}}\bigl(\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\eta_{\lambda})\bigr)=(y_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})
and
q_{\mathscr{K}}\bigl(\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)=(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})
for some
z_{\lambda},z\in X
. Since
\xi_{\lambda}\to\xi
, it follows that
y_{\lambda}\to y
. Since
\eta_{\lambda}\to\eta
and
q_{\mathscr{K}}\bigl(\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\eta_{\lambda})\bigr)=(y_{\lambda},z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})
, we further have that
(y_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda},h_{\lambda}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}z_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})\to q_{\mathscr{K}}(\eta)=(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}z^{\mathrm{op}})
. Since both
y_{\lambda}
and
y_{\lambda}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h_{\lambda}
converge, it follows from properness and freeness of the
\mathcal{H}
-action on
X
that a subnet of
\{h_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
converges to
h
; again, without loss of generality we can assume that the entire net converges. By continuity of
\Psi
, we conclude that
\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\eta_{\lambda})\to\Psi_{h}(\eta)
.
Since
U
is jointly continuous by Lemma
3.15
, we therefore have

U\bigl(\xi_{\lambda},\Psi_{h_{\lambda}}(\eta_{\lambda})\bigr)\longrightarrow U\bigl(\xi,\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)

in
K
, which suffices since
Q
is continuous.
∎

We arrive at our first main result:

Theorem 3.18
.

With respect to the multiplication in Lemma
3.16
and the involution in Corollary
3.10
, the upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{A}
described in Lemma
3.8
is a saturated Fell bundle over the groupoid
\mathcal{G}=X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
, called the imprimitivity Fell bundle of
\mathscr{M}
.

Proof.

We will check the conditions as stated in
[
4
, Definition 2.9]
.
It is clear that multiplication
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\cdot\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
is bilinear and that
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}^{*}
is conjugate linear and self-inverse; this takes care of Conditions (F2), (F6), and (F8). For the following, fix
([\xi],[\eta])\in\mathscr{A}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}
, and let
h\in\mathcal{H}
be the unique element such that
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
and
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\Psi_{h}(\eta))=(y,z^{\mathrm{op}})
, meaning that
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\eta)=(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}z^{\mathrm{op}})
.

(F1) Since
U_{y}\bigl(\xi,\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)\in K(x,z^{\mathrm{op}})
and since
p_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi])=[q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)]
, we get the first and last equality in the following computation:

p_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi]\cdot[\eta])=[x,z^{\mathrm{op}}]=[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\cdot[y,z^{\mathrm{op}}]=p_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi])\cdot p_{\mathscr{A}}([\eta]).

(F3) Associativity of the multiplication
can be shown using uniqueness of
h
, the second identity in (
3.20
), commutativity of Diagram
3.15
, and the fact that
\Psi_{hh^{\prime}}=\Psi_{h}\circ\Psi_{h^{\prime}}
; the details are left as an exercise.

(F4) The definition of the norm on the fibres of
\mathscr{A}
implies that

\|[\xi]\cdot[\eta]\|=\left\|U_{y}\bigl(\xi,\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)\right\|\overset{\ref{item:U:norm}}{\leq}\left\|\xi\right\|\,\left\|\Psi_{h}(\eta)\right\|\overset{\ref{item:Psi:isometric}}{=}\left\|\xi\right\|\,\left\|\eta\right\|=\left\|[\xi]\right\|\,\left\|[\eta]\right\|,

proving
that multiplication is norm-decreasing.

(F5)
Since
p_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi]^{*})=[q_{\mathscr{K}}(\mathtt{Flip}(\xi))]
and since
[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}=[y,x^{\mathrm{op}}]
in
\mathcal{G}
, we have that
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}^{*}
maps
A(g)
to
A(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})
.

(F7) We must show that
([\xi]\cdot[\eta])^{*}=[\eta]^{*}\cdot[\xi]^{*}
. We have

\displaystyle([\xi]\cdot[\eta])^{*}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathtt{Flip}\left(U_{y}\bigl(\xi,\Psi_{h}(\eta)\bigr)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[U_{y}\Bigl(\mathtt{Flip}(\Psi_{h}(\eta)),\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)\Bigr)\right]

by
(U4)

\displaystyle=\left[U_{y}\Bigl(\Psi_{h}(\mathtt{Flip}(\eta)),\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)\Bigr)\right]

by commutativity of Diagram (
3.11
)

\displaystyle=\left[U_{y}\Bigl(\mathtt{Flip}(\eta),\Psi_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}(\mathtt{Flip}(\xi))\Bigr)\right]

\displaystyle\text{by
\ref{item:U:cH equivariant} and definition of $\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}$},

which is exactly
[\eta]^{*}\cdot[\xi]^{*}
.

(F9) It is clear that
\|[\xi]\|=\|[\xi]^{*}\|
. To show
\|[\xi]\cdot[\xi]^{*}\|=\|[\xi]\|^{2}
, note that
\eta\coloneqq\mathtt{Flip}(\xi)
has

q_{\mathscr{K}}(\eta)=(y,x^{\mathrm{op}})
; in particular,
u\coloneqq\sigma(x)
is the unique element of
\mathcal{H}
with
\mathtt{sec}_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=\mathtt{fir}_{\mathscr{K}}\bigl(\Psi_{u}(\eta)\bigr)
. Since
\Psi_{u}
is the identity, we conclude that

[\xi]\cdot[\xi]^{*}=\bigl[U_{y}\bigl(\xi,\mathtt{Flip}(\xi))\bigr],

which has norm equal to
\left\|\xi\right\|^{2}=\left\|[\xi]\right\|^{2}
by
(U2)
, as needed.

(F10) To see that
[\xi]\cdot[\xi]^{*}\geq 0
, we
again invoke
[
RaWi:Morita
, Lemma 2.65]
: The matrix with
i,j
-entry
{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
is positive, so there exists a matrix
(b_{i,j})_{i,j}
over
B(u)
such that
{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}=\sum\nolimits_{l=1}b_{il}b_{jl}^{*}
. In particular, the
B(u)
-balancing in
M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
allows us to write

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n_{i}\penalty\mid\penalty n_{j}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})\otimes m_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}=\sum_{i,j,l}(m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(b_{il}b_{jl}^{*}))\otimes m_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}=\sum_{l}(\sum_{i}m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{il})\otimes(\sum_{j}m_{j}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{jl})^{\mathrm{op}}.

If we let
\mathbf{x}_{l}\coloneqq\sum_{i}m_{i}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b_{il}
, then this combined with (
3.17
) shows that

\displaystyle[\xi]\cdot[\xi]^{*}=\bigl[U_{y}\bigl(\xi,\mathtt{Flip}(\xi))\bigr]=\sum_{l}\left[\mathbf{x}_{l}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{l}^{\mathrm{op}}\right].

Since the element
\mathbf{x}_{l}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{l}^{\mathrm{op}}
of
K(x,x^{\mathrm{op}})
is positive (it corresponds to the positive operator
\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}_{l}\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.11543pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.50432pt}}_{{\kern-13.11543pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.50432pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}_{l}\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.11543pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.50432pt}}_{{\kern-13.11543pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.50432pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}_{l}\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.86499pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.25388pt}}_{{\kern-10.86499pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 11.25388pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert\mathbf{x}_{l}\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.01527pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.40416pt}}_{{\kern-10.01527pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.40416pt}}}\left\langle\mathbf{x}_{l}\right\rvert
in
\compacts_{B(u)}(M(x))
), and since the algebra
A([x,x^{\mathrm{op}}])
is *-isomorphic to
K(x,x^{\mathrm{op}})
(see Remark
3.17
), the claim follows.

Lastly, to see that
\mathscr{A}
is saturated, we must show that the linear span of elements of the form
[\xi]\cdot[\eta]
for
[\xi]\in A([x,y^{\mathrm{op}}])
and
[\eta]\in A([y,z^{\mathrm{op}}])
is dense in
A([x,z^{\mathrm{op}}])
. When we consider that
A([x,y^{\mathrm{op}}])\cong M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
and that these isomorphisms respect the multiplicative and linear structure we defined on
\mathscr{A}
, then the claim follows directly from the first isomorphism in Equation (
3.16
).
∎

4.
Equivalence from the imprimitivity Fell bundle to the ‘coefficient bundle’

We now proceed to equip the right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence
\mathscr{M}
with the structure of a left
\mathscr{A}
-demi-equivalence, where
\mathscr{A}=(p_{\mathscr{A}}\colon A\to\mathcal{G})
is the quotient of
\mathscr{K}=(K\to X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}})
by the equivalence relation
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
defined in Lemma
3.5
; we have shown in Theorem
3.18
that
\mathscr{A}
is a Fell bundle.

Proposition 4.1
.

There is a map
\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\colon A{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M\to M
given by

(4.1)

\displaystyle[\xi]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m\coloneqq\Phi_{x,y}\bigl(\Psi_{h}(\xi),m\bigr)

where
y=q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)
and where
(x,h)\in X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}
is the unique element such that
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\Psi_{h}(\xi))=(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})
. This map furthermore has the following properties.
6
6

6

The LA in “(LAn)” stands for “left action.”

(LA1)

It covers the map
\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}\makebox[7.15547pt]{\kern 0.71562pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.71562pt}\colon\mathcal{G}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X\to X
; and

(LA2)

[\xi]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)=([\xi]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b

for all appropriate
b\in B
.

Of course,
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}
will turn out to be an action in the sense of
[
4
, Definition 2.10]
; in particular, it is continuous, which one can indeed show by hand. But thanks to Proposition
2.4
, we can make do with proving fewer properties.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Write
p_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi])=[x_{1},x_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}]
. Since
([\xi],m)\in A{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.66995pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.02272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle s}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.32272pt{\scriptscriptstyle s}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M
, we have
s_{\mathcal{G}}([x_{1},x_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}])=\rho_{\mathscr{M}}(m)=[y,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
, which means that there exists a unique
h\in\mathcal{H}
such that

[x_{1},x_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}]=[x_{1}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
. Now that the second component is fixed as
y^{\mathrm{op}}
, the first component
x\coloneqq x_{1}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h
is also uniquely determined, and satisfies
\sigma(x)=s_{\mathcal{H}}(h)
. In other words, we have argued that the equality
s_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi])=\rho_{\mathscr{M}}(m)
implies that there exists a
unique
representative
\Psi_{h}(\xi)\in K
of the
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
-equivalence class
[\xi]
for which
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\Psi_{h}(\xi))
has
q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)^{\mathrm{op}}
as its second component:
\Psi_{h}(\xi)\in M(x)\otimes_{\sigma(x)}M(y^{\mathrm{op}})
.
We can now invoke Lemma
3.11
to conclude that
[\xi]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m
is a well-defined element of
M(x)
.

(LA1)
Follows from
p_{\mathscr{A}}([\xi])\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)=[x_{1},x_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}]\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y=[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y=x=q_{\mathscr{M}}([\xi]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m)
.

(LA2)
We compute

\displaystyle\Phi_{x,y}\bigl(m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}},k\bigr)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b=\left(m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b=m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\left({\mathchoice{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}b\right)=m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}=\Phi_{x,y}\bigl(m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}},k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\bigr),

so that bilinearity and continuity of
\Phi
implies

\Phi_{x,y}\bigl(\eta,k\bigr)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b=\Phi_{x,y}\bigl(\eta,k\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b\bigr)

for any appropriate
\eta\in K
. The claim follows.
∎

Proof.

The map is clearly well defined. Property
(LIP1)
follows immediately from Equation (
1.3
).

(LIP2)
Sesquilinearity is obvious. For continuity, it suffices to check that the map
M{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}M\to K
,
(m,n)\mapsto m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}
, is continuous since the quotient map
\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{A}
is continuous. But said continuity is built into the definition of
\mathscr{K}
’s topology; see Lemma
3.1
.

(LIP3)
Follows from the definition of the involution
∗
on
\mathscr{A}
.

(LIP4)

that

where

and where

, so that

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle[\xi]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty \displaystyle=\left[\Phi_{x,y}\bigl(\Psi_{h}(\xi),m_{1}\bigr)\otimes m_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[U\bigl(\Psi_{h}(\xi),m_{1}\otimes m_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)\right]

by Equation (

)

\displaystyle=[\xi]\cdot[m_{1}\otimes m_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}]=[\xi]\cdot{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-25.06996pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 23.79219pt}}_{{\kern-26.5283pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 23.79219pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-22.30042pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.02264pt}}_{{\kern-23.75876pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.02264pt}}}},

so
(LIP4)
holds.

(LIP5)
Let

and let

by

), and the second because the right

.

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-25.06996pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 23.79219pt}}_{{\kern-26.5283pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 23.79219pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-22.30042pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.02264pt}}_{{\kern-23.75876pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.02264pt}}}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}m_{3}
