Corollary 4.3
.

The saturated Fell bundle
\mathscr{A}
is equivalent to
\mathscr{B}
via
\mathscr{M}
with the
\mathscr{A}
-action and
\mathscr{A}
-inner product specified in Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
, respectively. In particular, the left action is continuous.

\blacktriangleright

For the remainder of the paper
, we will frequently denote the imprimitivity Fell bundle
\mathscr{A}
of
\mathscr{M}
by
\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
in analogy with
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
and with
\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
. However, the notation
\mathscr{A}=\compacts(\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{B}})
also lends itself well and has the advantage that it makes explicit reference to the underlying bundle
\mathscr{B}
(and the fact that it is acting on the right-hand side).

Proof of Corollary
4.3
.

We first check that
\mathscr{M}
is a left
\mathscr{A}
-demi-equivalence. In the following lists, all references are either to Proposition
4.1
or to Proposition
4.2
.

(DE1)

was shown in
(LA1)
;

(DE2)

was shown in
(LIP1)
;

(DE3)

was shown in
(LIP2)
;

(DE4)

was shown in
(LIP4)
; and

(DE5)

was shown in
(LIP3)
.

For the remaining conditions necessary for a demi-equivalence that we have not checked in any previous lemma, note that each
M(x)
is a full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-module by
(DE9)
, and so it is an imprimitivity bimodule between
M(x)\otimes_{u}M(x)^{\mathrm{op}}
and
B(u)
for
u\coloneqq\sigma(x)
. All structure with which we equipped the bundle
\mathscr{A}
is compatible with the homeomorphism
A(\rho(x))\cong M(x)\otimes_{u}M(x)^{\mathrm{op}}
in Lemma
3.7
; in other words, with the restriction to
M(x)
of the given
\mathscr{A}
-action and
\mathscr{A}
-inner product on
\mathscr{M}
, we recover exactly the structure that
M(x)
naturally carries. In particular:

(DE6)
:

We have
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-22.39095pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.11317pt}}_{{\kern-23.84929pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.11317pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-19.6214pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 18.34363pt}}_{{\kern-21.07974pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 18.34363pt}}}}\geq 0
in the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A(\rho_{\mathscr{M}}(m))
, and
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-22.39095pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.11317pt}}_{{\kern-23.84929pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.11317pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-19.6214pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 18.34363pt}}_{{\kern-21.07974pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 18.34363pt}}}}=0
only if
m=0
.

(DE7)
:

By
[
RaWi:Morita
, Lemma 2.30.]
, the norm of
{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
in
B(\sigma(x))
coincides with the norm of
m\otimes m^{\mathrm{op}}
in
M(x)\otimes_{u}M(x)^{\mathrm{op}}
which, by definition, is exactly the norm of
[m\otimes m^{\mathrm{op}}]={\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-22.39095pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.11317pt}}_{{\kern-23.84929pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.11317pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-19.6214pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 18.34363pt}}_{{\kern-21.07974pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 18.34363pt}}}}
in
A(\rho(x))
. Since
\mathscr{M}
is a right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence, the norm
m\mapsto\left\|{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|^{1/2}
agrees with the norm that the upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{M}
already carries, which is therefore in turn identical to the norm
m\mapsto\left\|{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-29.30089pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 27.55644pt}}_{{\kern-30.29256pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 27.55644pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-22.39095pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.11317pt}}_{{\kern-23.84929pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.11317pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{\kern-19.6214pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 18.34363pt}}_{{\kern-21.07974pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 18.34363pt}}}}\right\|^{1/2}
.

(DE8)
:

For each
x\in X
, the linear span of
\{{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-33.3799pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 31.63545pt}}_{{\kern-34.37157pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 31.63545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-25.06996pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 23.79219pt}}_{{\kern-26.5283pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 23.79219pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}}}}\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{\kern-22.30042pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 21.02264pt}}_{{\kern-23.75876pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}}\kern 21.02264pt}}}}:m_{i}\in M(x)\}
is dense in
A(\rho(x))
, since
M(x)
is an imprimitivity bimodule.

That
A(\rho(x))\cong M(x)\otimes_{u}M(x)^{\mathrm{op}}
further implies that
Assumption
(2)
of Proposition
2.4
is satisfied. Since Assumptions
(1)
and
(3)
were shown in
(LA2)
and
(LIP5)
, respectively, we can conclude that
\mathscr{M}
is an equivalence by Proposition
2.4
.
∎

5.
The imprimitivity Fell bundle: Uniqueness

For the next result, we remind the reader of Remark
1.1
: given a
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
-groupoid equivalence
X
and two elements
x,y\in X
with the same
\mathcal{H}
-anchor, we write
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
for the unique element of
\mathcal{G}
that satisfies
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.95778pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-23.51964pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-21.67175pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y=x
.

Proposition 5.1

(cf.
[
1
, Corollary 3.11]
)
.

Suppose
\mathscr{A}=(A\to\mathcal{G})
,
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}=(A^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, and
\mathscr{B}=(B\to\mathcal{H})
are Fell bundles over groupoids
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}
respectively, and that
\mathscr{M}=(q_{\mathscr{M}}\colon M\to X)
is both an
(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})
- and an
(\mathscr{A}^{\prime},\mathscr{B})
-Fell bundle equivalence. Then there exists a unique Fell bundle isomorphism
\Omega\colon\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
determined by the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

By Corollary
4.3
, we may without loss of generality assume that
\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
and
\mathcal{G}=X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
. In that setting,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)\penalty\mid\penalty q_{\mathscr{M}}(n)^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-66.8996pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 63.61418pt}}_{{\kern-66.47182pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 63.61418pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)\penalty\mid\penalty q_{\mathscr{M}}(n)^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-66.8996pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 63.61418pt}}_{{\kern-66.47182pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 63.61418pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)\penalty\mid\penalty q_{\mathscr{M}}(n)^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-45.57747pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 42.29205pt}}_{{\kern-45.14969pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 42.29205pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}}}}\left\{q_{\mathscr{M}}(m)\penalty\mid\penalty q_{\mathscr{M}}(n)^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-39.66864pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 36.38322pt}}_{{\kern-39.24086pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}}\kern 36.38322pt}}}
is exactly
[q_{\mathscr{M}}(m),q_{\mathscr{M}}(n)]\in\mathcal{G}
; see Lemma
1.3
. We also remind the reader that
\omega\colon X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime},[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-34.42027pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-34.42027pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-23.94742pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 20.662pt}}_{{\kern-24.98213pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\kern 20.662pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-22.09953pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 18.81412pt}}_{{\kern-23.13425pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\kern 18.81412pt}}}
, is known to be an isomorphism of topological groupoids. It is therefore clear that
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}\cong\omega^{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\prime})
, and so we may without loss of generality assume that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}
(and hence
\omega=\operatorname{id}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}=\omega^{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\prime})
). As before, we will denote the anchor maps of
X
by
\sigma\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
and
\rho\colon X\to\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}},x\mapsto[x,x^{\mathrm{op}}]
. Recall that
\mathcal{G}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}\cong X/\mathcal{H}
via
[x,x^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathcal{H}
.

To construct
\Omega
, we will first construct a surjective bundle map

\tilde{\Omega}\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
covering the quotient map
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{G},(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
. We will then show that

\tilde{\Omega}
is constant on
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
-equivalence classes
(where
\mathbin{\mathtt{R}}
is as in Lemma
3.5
), so that
\tilde{\Omega}
induces a bundle map
\Omega
from the quotient
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}=Q(\mathscr{K})
to
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
covering the identity map
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}
. After we have shown that
\Omega
is injective, we will then prove the conditions in
[
4
, Propositions A.7 and A.8]
to deduce that
\Omega
is open and continuous and hence the claimed isomorphism of Fell bundles.

We define
\tilde{\Omega}
fibrewise. Fix
u\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
and
x,y\in Xu
, let
g\coloneqq[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
, and consider

(5.1)

M(x)\times M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}_{g},\quad(m,n^{\mathrm{op}})\mapsto{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-29.60226pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-29.60226pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-24.23816pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 19.75206pt}}_{{\kern-23.95067pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 19.75206pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-21.8575pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 17.3714pt}}_{{\kern-21.57pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 17.3714pt}}}}.

, meaning that the map in (

\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}\colon M(x)\odot_{B(u)}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\to A^{\prime}(g),\quad\text{determined by}\quad\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}(m\odot n^{\mathrm{op}})={\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-29.60226pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-29.60226pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-24.23816pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 19.75206pt}}_{{\kern-23.95067pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 19.75206pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-21.8575pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 17.3714pt}}_{{\kern-21.57pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 17.3714pt}}}},

We claim that
\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
has dense range. Since
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
is a Fell bundle,
A^{\prime}(g)
is an
A^{\prime}(x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathcal{H})-A^{\prime}(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathcal{H})
-imprimitivity bimodule, so by

, Proposition 2.33 (Hewitt-Cohen)]
, any element of
A^{\prime}(g)
can be written as

for some

and some

, where the product is the multiplication in the Fell bundle
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
. By assumption,
M(y)
is a
A^{\prime}(y\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}\mathcal{H})-B(u)
-imprimitivity bimodule. In particular,
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle M(y)\penalty\mid\penalty M(y)\rangle^{{\kern-56.18335pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 51.23058pt}}_{{\kern-55.42918pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 51.23058pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle M(y)\penalty\mid\penalty M(y)\rangle^{{\kern-56.18335pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 51.23058pt}}_{{\kern-55.42918pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 51.23058pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle M(y)\penalty\mid\penalty M(y)\rangle^{{\kern-40.34909pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 35.86299pt}}_{{\kern-40.0616pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 35.86299pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle M(y)\penalty\mid\penalty M(y)\rangle^{{\kern-33.36531pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 28.87921pt}}_{{\kern-33.07782pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 28.87921pt}}}}

,

, and

,

. For an arbitrary but fixed element

we have

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle \displaystyle\text{by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DL:MJM2023}{}{}, Corollary 4.6]}}.

Since
\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}(\Psi_{h}(\xi))=\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}(\xi)
for all
\xi
in the algebraic tensor product, we conclude that
\tilde{\Omega}
descends to a bundle map

\Omega\colon\quad\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime},\quad\text{determined by}\quad\Omega([m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}])={\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-29.60226pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-30.35643pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 25.40366pt}}_{{\kern-29.60226pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 25.40366pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-24.23816pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 19.75206pt}}_{{\kern-23.95067pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 19.75206pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty n\rangle^{{\kern-21.8575pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 17.3714pt}}_{{\kern-21.57pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 17.3714pt}}}},

covering the identity map
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}
.
We must now show that this map is a continuous open bijection.

Since
\Omega
covers the identity map, surjectivity of
\Omega
follows from the fact that each
\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
has dense range, and injectivity of
\Omega
follows since each
\tilde{\Omega}_{(x,y^{\mathrm{op}})}
is isometric. To see that
\Omega
is continuous, suppose that we have a convergent net in
\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
, say
[\xi_{\lambda}]\to[\xi]
; by
(2)\implies(1)
in
[
23
, Theorem 18.1.]
, it suffices to show that a subnet of
\{\Omega([\xi_{\lambda}])\}_{\lambda}
converges to
\Omega([\xi])
. Since the quotient map
Q\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{A}
is open (Lemma
3.5
), we can without loss of generality assume that
\xi_{\lambda}\to\xi
in
\mathscr{K}
. Let
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi_{\lambda})=(x_{\lambda},y_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{op}})
with limit
q_{\mathscr{K}}(\xi)=(x,y)
in
X{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X^{\mathrm{op}}
. We now invoke the definition of the topology on
\mathscr{K}
(Lemma
3.1
; see also
[
4
, Lemma A.3]
): for any given
\epsilon>0
, we can find finitely many continuous sections
\mu_{i},\nu_{i}
of
\mathscr{M}
and a
\lambda_{0}
such that for all
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, we have

(5.2)

\left\|\xi_{\lambda}-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\otimes\nu_{i}(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|<\epsilon\text{ and }\left\|\xi-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mu_{i}(x)\otimes\nu_{i}(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\right\|<\epsilon.

The norm on each fibre
A([x,y^{\mathrm{op}}])
of
\mathscr{A}
is defined as the norm on one of its lifts under
Q
, meaning that
Q
restricted to
M(x)\otimes_{u}M(y)^{\mathrm{op}}
is isometric. Since
\Omega
is likewise isometric, (
5.2
) implies

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left\|\Omega([\xi_{\lambda}])-\Omega\left(\left[\sum\nolimits_{i}\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\otimes\nu_{i}(y_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{op}}\right]\right)\right\|<\epsilon\text{ and }\left\|\Omega([\xi])-\Omega\left(\left[\sum\nolimits_{i}\mu_{i}(x)\otimes\nu_{i}(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\right]\right)\right\|<\epsilon.

By definition of
\Omega
,

\Omega\left(\left[\sum\nolimits_{i}\mu_{i}(x)\otimes\nu_{i}(y)^{\mathrm{op}}\right]\right)=\sum\nolimits_{i}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-52.90634pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 47.95357pt}}_{{\kern-52.15218pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 47.95357pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-52.90634pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 47.95357pt}}_{{\kern-52.15218pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 47.95357pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-38.78018pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 34.29408pt}}_{{\kern-38.49269pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 34.29408pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-33.2194pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 28.7333pt}}_{{\kern-32.93192pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 28.7333pt}}}}.

By assumption on
\mathscr{M}
,
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-25.83609pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 20.88332pt}}_{{\kern-25.08192pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 20.88332pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-25.83609pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 20.88332pt}}_{{\kern-25.08192pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 20.88332pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-20.21274pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 15.72664pt}}_{{\kern-19.92525pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 15.72664pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{\kern-17.94164pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 13.45554pt}}_{{\kern-17.65414pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 13.45554pt}}}}
is continuous, so that continuity of
\mu_{i},\nu_{i}
and of addition in
\mathscr{A}
implies

\sum\nolimits_{i}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y_{\lambda})\rangle^{{\kern-59.62302pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 54.67024pt}}_{{\kern-58.86885pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 54.67024pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y_{\lambda})\rangle^{{\kern-59.62302pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 54.67024pt}}_{{\kern-58.86885pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 54.67024pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y_{\lambda})\rangle^{{\kern-43.86353pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 39.37743pt}}_{{\kern-43.57603pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 39.37743pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x_{\lambda})\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y_{\lambda})\rangle^{{\kern-38.30275pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 33.81665pt}}_{{\kern-38.01526pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 33.81665pt}}}}\to\sum\nolimits_{i}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-52.90634pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 47.95357pt}}_{{\kern-52.15218pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 47.95357pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-52.90634pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 47.95357pt}}_{{\kern-52.15218pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 47.95357pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-38.78018pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 34.29408pt}}_{{\kern-38.49269pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 34.29408pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}}}}\langle\mu_{i}(x)\penalty\mid\penalty\nu_{i}(y)\rangle^{{\kern-33.2194pt{\hfill\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}\kern 28.7333pt}}_{{\kern-32.93192pt{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\kern 28.7333pt}}}}.

Combining (
5.3
) with Lemma
B.1
, we conclude that
\Omega([\xi_{\lambda}])\to\Omega([\xi])
, so
\Omega
is continuous. Lastly, since
\Omega
is isometric, it follows from
[
4
, Proposition A.8]
that
\Omega
is open as well.
∎

This also finishes the proof of the main theorem, Theorem
1.6
, since it is a combination of Corollary
4.3
and Proposition
5.1
.

Remark 5.2
.

A brief comment on another property related to equivalences that has a version not only in the realm of groupoids and
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras but also of Fell bundles: linking objects.

By
[
RaWi:Morita
, Theorem 3.19.]
, two
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras are strongly Morita equivalent if and only if they are complementary full corners of another algebra, called the
linking algebra
. In
[
27
, Theorem 4.1]
, this result was moved into groupoid-land: an equivalence of groupoids gives rise to a
linking groupoid
whose
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra is the linking algebra that witnesses the strong Morita equivalence. Likewise, a Fell bundle equivalence has a
linking (Fell) bundle
(see
[
26
, Section 3]
,
[
1
, Theorem 3.2]
) whose
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra is the linking algebra of the Fell bundle
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras
[
26
, Theorem 14]
.

It would be interesting to study whether ‘one-sided’ versions of these linking objects exist. In the paper at hand, we considered principal groupoid-spaces
X
, full right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules
\mathbf{X}
, and Fell bundle-demi-equivalences
\mathscr{M}
, and turned them into equivalences between the coefficient object and the ‘generalized compacts’, that is, the imprimitivity groupoid
X\times_{\mathcal{H}}X^{\mathrm{op}}
, the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra of compact operators
\compacts(\mathbf{X}_{A})
, and the imprimitivity Fell bundle
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}=\compacts(\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{B}})
, respectively. Do these results have analogues in the language of linking objects?

6.
Applications

The original impetus for the paper at hand was Proposition A.2.3 in the appendix of
[
19
]
; it can easily seen to be a corollary of our main theorem in the special case that
\mathcal{H}
is a group that acts freely and properly on a space
X
. Before we study other interesting applications, let us first do some sanity checks.
In analogy to the isomorphism of imprimitivity groupoids in Example
1.2
, we have:

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
\mathscr{B}
be a Fell bundle, considered as a self-Fell bundle equivalence
[
22
, Example 6.6]
. Then there is an isomorphism of Fell bundles
\compacts(\mathscr{B}_{\mathscr{B}})=\mathscr{B}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{B}^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathscr{B}
determined by
[b_{1}\otimes b_{2}^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto b_{1}\cdot b_{2}^{*}
.

In analogy to the isomorphism of Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bimodules in (
3.16
), we have:

Corollary 6.2
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{M}
,
\mathscr{N}
,
\mathscr{K}
are right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalences, and let
\mathscr{A}=\compacts(\mathscr{N}_{\mathscr{B}})
be the imprimitivity Fell bundle of
\mathscr{N}
. Then
(\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathscr{N}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}})\cong\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}
as upper semi-continuous Banach bundles.

Proof sketch.

First, let us make sure that the objects we have written down are not nonsense: By Theorem
1.6
, all three demi-equivalences are equivalences between their respective imprimitivity Fell bundles and
\mathscr{B}
. As explained in
[
22
, Example 6.7]
, their opposites are then likewise equivalences, just in the other direction. It was shown in
[
4
]
that equivalences can be concatenated:
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}}
is an equivalence from the imprimitivity Fell bundle of
\mathscr{M}
to
\mathscr{A}
, and so forth.
In particular, both the left- and the right-hand side of the alleged isomorphism is an equivalence between the imprimitivity Fell bundle of
\mathscr{M}
and that of
\mathscr{K}
.

Since the balanced tensor product of imprimitivity bimodule is associative, it is easy to see that the same is true for
\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}
of Fell bundle equivalences. In particular,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathscr{N}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\cong\bigl(\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}(\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{N})\bigr)\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}.

By Proposition
5.1
(applied to
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}}
), the Fell bundle
\compacts(\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathscr{A}})=\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{N}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{B}
. Since the balanced tensor product of imprimitivity bimodule absorbs the coefficient algebra (meaning that
\mathbf{X}\otimes_{A}A\cong\mathbf{X}
), it is easy to see that
\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}
absorbs the coefficient Fell bundle. Thus,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{op}})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathscr{N}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\cong(\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{B})\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}},

as claimed.
∎

We can also conclude some other, well-known results.

Corollary 6.3
.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
is a locally compact Hausdorff groupoid with Haar system
\{\lambda_{u}\}_{u\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}
and
\mathscr{B}
is a Fell bundle over
\mathcal{H}
. Then
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathscr{B})
is strongly Morita equivalent to
\mathrm{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathscr{B}))
.

Note that the right choice of
\mathscr{B}
allows
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathscr{B})
to model the full
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras of groupoids (with or without a twist á la Kumjian) and full crossed product
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras.

Proof.

Take
X=\mathcal{H}
, with
\sigma\colon X\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}
the source map of
\mathcal{H}
. The existence of a Haar system implies that the source map of
\mathcal{H}
(and hence the anchor map of
X
) is open
[
28
, Proposition 1.23]
, so we are in good shape to use our main theorem, provided we can find the right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence. Let
\mathscr{M}
to be the bundle over
X
with fibres
\mathbb{C}^{n}\times\mathscr{B}(h)
for each
h\in\mathcal{H}
. We define

\displaystyle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\colon

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{M}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{B}\to\mathscr{M},\quad(\vec{x},b)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b^{\prime}\coloneqq(\vec{x},b\cdot b^{\prime}),

and

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\colon

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{M}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.02927pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.38203pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.68204pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\sigma}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathscr{M}\to\mathscr{B},\quad{\mathchoice{\langle(\vec{x},b)\penalty\mid\penalty(\vec{y},c)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle(\vec{x},b)\penalty\mid\penalty(\vec{y},c)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle(\vec{x},b)\penalty\mid\penalty(\vec{y},c)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle(\vec{x},b)\penalty\mid\penalty(\vec{y},c)\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\langle\vec{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\vec{y}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}^{n}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}}}}}{\langle\vec{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\vec{y}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}^{n}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}}}}}{\langle\vec{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\vec{y}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}^{n}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}}}}}{\langle\vec{x}\penalty\mid\penalty\vec{y}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}^{n}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C}}}}}}\,b^{*}\cdot c,

where we choose the inner product on
\mathbb{C}^{n}
to be conjugate linear in the first coordinate to match up with our definitions of Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules.
Then, using Corollary
6.1
, it is easy to see that

\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathrm{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathscr{B}\text{ via }[(\vec{e}_{i},b_{1})\otimes(\vec{e}_{j},b_{2})^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto(E_{i,j},b_{1}\cdot b_{2}^{*}),

where
\vec{e}_{i}
is the standard basis vector of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
E_{i,j}
is the matrix unit with a
1
in the
i^{\text{th}}
row and
j^{\text{th}}
column and
0
s everywhere else. By Theorem
1.6
, the bundles
\mathscr{B}
and
\mathrm{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathscr{B}
are equivalent. By

[
22
, Theorem 6.4]
, it follows that
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathscr{B})
is strongly Morita equivalent to
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathrm{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathscr{B})\cong\mathrm{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathscr{B}))
.
∎

6.1.
Kumjian’s Stabilization trick

In this subsection, we will see that our main theorem implies
[
17
, Corollary 4.5]
, which in its original form was only shown for principal
r
-discrete groupoids. The approach here is inspired by that preceding
[
20
, Theorem 15]
. We will go through it in great detail to help the reader understand our technical results.

We start with a (saturated) Fell bundle
\mathscr{B}=(B\to\mathcal{H})
over a locally compact Hausdorff groupoid
\mathcal{H}
with Haar system
\{\lambda_{v}\}_{v\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}
. Fix
h\in\mathcal{H}
, and let
v\coloneqq r(h)
and
u\coloneqq s(h)
. We define
M_{0}(h)\coloneqq\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{H}u;\mathscr{B})
, sections of the restriction of
\mathscr{B}
to
\mathcal{H}u=s^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}(s(h))
. If
g\in\mathcal{H}
is another element with
r(g)=v
, then we define a sesquilinear form

{\mathchoice{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}{\langle\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\penalty\mid\penalty\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}}\colon M_{0}(g)\times M_{0}(h)\to B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)

by

(6.1)

{\mathchoice{\langle\mu\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}{\langle\mu\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}{\langle\mu\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}{\langle\mu\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)}}}}}=\int_{\mathcal{H}s(g)}\mu(\ell)^{*}\xi(\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\lambda_{s(g)}(\ell).

Note that this indeed makes sense: if
\ell\in\mathcal{H}s(g)
, then
\ell
is in the domain of
\mu
and
\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}
is defined. Moreover,
s(\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)=s(h)
, so
\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h
is in the domain of
\xi
. Since
\mu(\ell)^{*}\in B(\ell^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})
and
\xi(\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)\in B(\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)
, their product is indeed an element of
B(\ell^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\ell g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)=B(g^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h)
. And since
\mu
and
\xi
are compactly supported, the integral exists.

For
h=g
, the form is valued in the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
B(u)
, so we may take the completion
M(h)
of
M_{0}(h)
with respect to the induced norm

\left\|\xi\right\|_{M(h)}\coloneqq\left\|{\mathchoice{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle B(u)}}}}}\right\|^{1/2}.

If
k\in u\mathcal{H}w
, so that
(h,k)\in\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(2)$}}
, then the fibre
B(k)
can act on an element
\xi
of
M_{0}(h)
and deliver an element
\xi\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b
of
M_{0}(hk)
: if
\ell
is in
\mathcal{H}w
(the domain of any element of
M_{0}(hk)
), then
\ell k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\in\mathcal{H}u
is in the domain of
\xi
and so

(\xi\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}b)(\ell)\coloneqq\xi(\ell k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})\cdot b\text{ is defined and an element of }B(\ell k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})\cdot B(k)\subseteq B(\ell).

This extends to a map
M(h)\times B(k)\to M(hk)
. In fact, it induces an isomorphism
M(h)\otimes_{u}B(k)\cong M(hk)
of right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
B(w)
-modules.

We let
\mathscr{M}=(q_{\mathscr{M}}\colon M\to\mathcal{H})
be the bundle with fibres
M(h)
, which we will now topologize.
If
\tau\in\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{H};\mathscr{B})
is a section of
\mathscr{B}
and
h\in\mathcal{H}u
, is arbitrary then
\tilde{\tau}(h)\coloneqq\tau|_{\mathcal{H}u}
is a continuous, compactly supported section
\mathcal{H}u\to\mathscr{B}
; i.e.,
\tilde{\tau}(h)
is an element of
M(h)
, so
\tilde{\tau}
is a section of the bundle
\mathscr{M}
. The family

\{\tilde{\tau}\colon\tau\in\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{H};\mathscr{B})\}

uniquely induces a topology on
\mathscr{M}
making it upper semi-continuous. The attentive reader will have expected what comes next: we will show that
\mathscr{M}
is a right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence over the principal
\mathcal{H}
-space
X=\mathcal{H}
(Example
1.2
).

Remark 6.4
.

Let us compare what we have done so far with what was done in
[
20
,
17
]
; this will also give us an indicator as to what will happen next.
[
20
]
constructs first an upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{V}=(V\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}})
, which is exactly
\mathscr{V}=\mathscr{M}|_{\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}
and which
[

]
calls the “Hilbert
\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}

\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}

; he points out that

From

; in the notation of the paper at hand,
\mathscr{E}
can be constructed as the pullback bundle of

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}\mathscr{B}=\left(\bigsqcup_{(v,h)}V(v)\otimes_{v}B(h)\to\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-7.29875pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-6.59875pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H}\right)

along the isomorphism
\mathcal{H}\cong\mathcal{H}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-8.94598pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-7.29875pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-6.59875pt{\scriptscriptstyle\operatorname{id}}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle r}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathcal{H},h\mapsto(r(h),h)
.

By construction

(DE3)

Follows from the properties of Haar systems (Property (HS2) in

(DE4)
Straight-forward computation.

(DE5)

Follows from the properties of Haar systems (Property (HS3) in

(DE6)

Since the integrand of
{\mathchoice{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}

, so does the inner product itself, and
{\mathchoice{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\xi\penalty\mid\penalty\xi\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}=0
implies

for all

, which is the entire domain of
\xi
.

(DE7)
Holds by definition of the Banach space structure on

(DE8)
Note that

It follows from Theorem

.

In view of

A(h)\coloneqq M(v)\otimes_{v}M(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\text{ for }h\in\mathcal{H}^{v}_{u}.

To describe the multiplication map, we must be a bit careful: if

, then our choice of section turns the composable pair

into

\displaystyle A(h)\times A(k)

\displaystyle=\left(M(v)\otimes_{v}M(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\right)\times\left(M(u)\otimes_{u}M(k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\right).

On the level of groupoids, we must therefore replace (for example) the representative
(u,(k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}})
by
(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}},(k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}})

Having found compatible representatives, we can then use the

-map from Lemma

:

\displaystyle A(h)\times A(k)

\displaystyle\overset{\operatorname{id}\times\Psi_{h}}{\longrightarrow}\left(M(v)\otimes_{v}M(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\right)\times\left(M(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})\otimes_{v}M(k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\right)

\displaystyle\ni\phantom{\mapsto}(\xi\otimes\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{op}},\eta_{2}\otimes\zeta^{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle\overset{U_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}}{\longrightarrow}M(v)\otimes_{v}M(k^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}=A(hk)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\xi\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle\eta_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\eta_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\eta_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\eta_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\eta_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\eta_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle\eta_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty\eta_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}})\otimes\zeta^{\mathrm{op}}.

We therefore see that, with the choice of
A(h)
that we have made, the multiplication
A(h)\times A(k)\to A(hk)
is given by
U_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times\Psi_{h})
.
The involution on
A
is easier to decipher: it is given by

A(h)=M(v)\otimes_{v}M(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\mathtt{Flip}}{\longrightarrow}M(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})\otimes_{v}M(v)^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\Psi_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}}{\longrightarrow}M(u)\otimes_{u}M(h)^{\mathrm{op}}=A(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}).

Now,
M(h)
and
M(s(h))
are
equal
as right-Hilbert
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-modules; the letter
h
is merely needed to keep track of the ‘mixed’ inner product in Equation (
6.1
).
This means that we have an isomorphism

\Psi_{h}\colon\qquad V(u)\otimes_{u}V(u)^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow V(v)\otimes_{u}V(v)^{\mathrm{op}}.

If we make the canonical identification of
V(u)\otimes_{u}V(u)^{\mathrm{op}}
with
\compacts\bigl(V(u)_{B(u)}\bigr)=\compacts(\mathscr{V}_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}})(s(h))
, then the above can be written as

h\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\colon\qquad\compacts(\mathscr{V}_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}})(s(h))\longrightarrow\compacts(\mathscr{V}_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}})(r(h)).

In other words,
\Psi
encodes an action of
\mathcal{H}
on the bundle
\compacts(\mathscr{V}_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}})
which covers the map
\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}\makebox[6.99997pt]{\kern 0.70007pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=1.29167pt\kern 0.70007pt}\colon h\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}s(h)=r(h)
.
In this picture, the map
\mathtt{Flip}
is just the adjoint:

If we write

A(h)=[V(v)\times\{v\}]\otimes_{v}[V(v)\times\{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}\}]^{\mathrm{op}}.=[V(v)\otimes_{v}V(v)^{\mathrm{op}}]\times\{h\}\cong\compacts\bigl(V(v)_{B(v)}\bigr)\times\{h\},

then
as sets,
\mathscr{A}=\compacts\bigl(\mathscr{V}_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}\bigr)\rtimes\mathcal{H}
. Moreover, we found that the multiplication
A(h)\times A(k)\to A(hk)
is given by
U_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times\Psi_{h})
and the involution
A(h)\to A(h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}})
by
\Psi_{h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}}\circ\mathtt{Flip}
, which we have shown translates to

(T_{1},h)\cdot(T_{2},k)=\bigl(T_{1}\circ(h\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}T_{2}),hk\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad(T,h)^{*}=(T^{*},h^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}),

which are exactly the formulas in the semi-direct product.
We deduce that the imprimitivity Fell bundle
\mathscr{A}
of the
\mathscr{B}
-action on
\mathscr{M}
is exactly the Fell bundle
\compacts\bigl(\mathscr{V}_{\mathscr{B}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}}\bigr)\rtimes\mathcal{H}
, showing that Theorem
1.6
recovers
13
13

13

Of course, there is still the difficulty of having to come up with a suitable
\mathscr{M}
in the first place; we have taken it for granted here.

[
17
]
’s Stabilization trick.

6.2.
Higher order operators
à la
Abadie–Ferraro

As alluded to earlier, a theorem similar to our main theorem has appeared in
[
1
]
in the setting that
\mathcal{H}=X=\mathcal{G}
is a group. Before we can cite it, let us first establish the following bridge between their terminology and ours:

Lemma 6.5
.

Suppose
\mathscr{B}
is a saturated Fell bundle over a locally compact Hausdorff group
\mathcal{H}
. Then
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalences over the same group are exactly right Hilbert
\mathscr{B}
-bundles in the sense of
[
1
, Definition 2.1]
that are fibrewise full.

Proof.

A priori
, the norm of a demi-equivalence
\mathscr{M}=(M\to\mathcal{H})
is only upper semi-continuous. However, since
\mathscr{B}
is a Fell bundle over a group, it follows from
[
2
, Lemma 3.30]
that
b\mapsto\left\|b\right\|
is continuous (not only upper semi-continuous) on
\mathscr{B}
, and since the norm on
\mathscr{M}
is given by
\left\|{\mathchoice{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m\penalty\mid\penalty m\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\right\|^{1/2}
by Assumption
(DE7)
, it is the concatenation of continuous maps and hence itself continuous. Therefore, the upper semi-continuous Banach bundle
\mathscr{M}
is actually a
continuous
Banach bundle in the sense of
[
9
, Definition II.13.4]
, as needed for
[
1
, Definition 2.1]
.

As in
[
1
]
, the inner product and the right-action are continuous by
(DE3)
and
(DE16)
, respectively. The assumption that the Hilbert bundle be fibrewise full corresponds to
(DE8)
(and is stronger than the assumption (7R) in
[
1
]
). The remaining items of
[
1
, Definition 2.1]
correspond to our assumptions as follows.

(1R)

corresponds to
(DE1)
and
(DE2)
, using that
\mathchoice{\left\{h_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty h_{2}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{h_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty h_{2}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{h_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty h_{2}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}{\left\{h_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty h_{2}\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}}}}}=h_{1}^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}h_{2}
as explained in Example
1.2
;

(2R)

corresponds to
(DE13)
;

(3R)

corresponds to
(DE3)
;

(4R)

corresponds to
(DE4)
and
(DE5)
;

(5R)

corresponds to
(DE6)
; and

(6R)

corresponds to
(DE7)
. ∎

We can now restate the result of
[
1
]
to which we want to compare ours, in our terminology. We will artificially add the assumption that the fibres are all full, to align with our situation. We will further use different letters for elements of the group
\mathcal{H}
: we use
x
if we think of
\mathcal{H}
as a principal
\mathcal{H}
-space; we use
h
if we think of
\mathcal{H}
as the group acting on the right, and
g
if it is acting on the left; in particular,
x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h
and
g\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}x
will just be the products
xh
respectively
gx
in
\mathcal{H}
.

Theorem 6.6

(
[
1
, Definition 3.6, Theorem 3.9, Corollary 3.10]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a locally compact Hausdorff group,
\mathscr{B}=(B\to\mathcal{H})
a Fell bundle, and
\mathscr{M}=(M\to\mathcal{H})
a right
\mathscr{B}
-demi-equivalence, also over
\mathcal{H}
. For
g\in\mathcal{H}
, let
\mathbb{B}_{g}(\mathscr{M})
be the collection of continuous maps
S\colon M\to M
such that

•

there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\left\|Sm\right\|\leq c\left\|m\right\|
for all
m\in M
;

•

S(M(x))\subseteq M(g\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}x)
for all
x\in\mathcal{H}
; and

•

there exists
S^{*}\colon M\to M
such that
{\mathchoice{\langle Sm_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle Sm_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle Sm_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle Sm_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}={\mathchoice{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty S^{*}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty S^{*}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty S^{*}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle m_{1}\penalty\mid\penalty S^{*}m_{2}\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
for all
m_{i}\in M
.

Then there exists a unique Fell bundle
\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})
over
\mathcal{H}
such
that:

(i)

for all
g\in\mathcal{H}
, the fiber
\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})_{g}
is, as a Banach space, the closure in
\mathbb{B}_{g}(\mathscr{M})
of

\mathrm{span}\{\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}_{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}_{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}}\left\langle n\right\rvert:m\in M(g\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}x),n\in M(x),x\in\mathcal{H}\}

where
\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}_{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}_{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}}\left\langle n\right\rvert\colon M\to M,k\mapsto m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}
;

(ii)

given
\psi,\varphi\in C_{c}(M)
and
x\in\mathcal{H}
, the function
[\psi,\varphi,x]\colon\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})
given by

[\psi,\varphi,x](g)=[\psi(g\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}x),\varphi(x)]
, is a continuous section of
\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})
.

Moreover,
\mathscr{M}
is a
\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})-\mathscr{B}
-equivalence
bundle with the action
\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})\times M\to M
given by
(S,m)\to S(m)
and the left inner
product
M\times M\to\mathbb{K}(\mathscr{M})
given by
(m,n)\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}_{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}_{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}}\left\langle n\right\rvert
.

[
1
]
call the elements of
\mathbb{B}_{g}(\mathscr{M})

adjointable operators of order
g
. We conclude:

Corollary 6.7
.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
,
\mathscr{B}
, and
\mathscr{M}
are as in Theorem
6.6
. The map

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\compacts(\mathscr{M})

which maps
[m\otimes n^{\mathrm{op}}]
in the fibre over
[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]
of
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
to the operator

\mathchoice{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}_{{\kern-13.37392pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 13.7628pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}_{{\kern-10.58006pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 10.96895pt}}}{\hphantom{}\left\lvert m\right\rangle^{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}_{{\kern-9.35085pt{\scriptscriptstyle}\kern 9.73973pt}}}\left\langle n\right\rvert\colon\quad M\ni k\mapsto m\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.9pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.6pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathbin{\raisebox{-0.2pt}{$\includegraphics[scale,angle,origin]{semithickHleftB.pdf}$}}}{\mathchoice{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}{\langle n\penalty\mid\penalty k\rangle^{{{{\mskip-2.0mu}\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{B}}}}}}\in M

in the fibre over
xy^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}
of
\compacts(\mathscr{M})
, is an isomorphism of Fell bundles, covering the map
f
from Example
1.2
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.5
,
\mathscr{M}
is a right Hilbert
\mathscr{B}
-bundle in the sense of
[
1
, Definition 2.1]
.
It is proven in
[
1
, Corollary 3.10]
that
\mathscr{M}
is an equivalence between
\mathscr{B}
and
\compacts(\mathscr{M})
. Since
\mathscr{M}
is also an equivalence between
\mathscr{B}
and
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
, it thus follows from
[
1
, Corollary 3.11]
(or equivalently, from Proposition
5.1
), that the displayed map is the claimed isomorphism.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

As alluded to in Remark
2.5
, the description
à la

[
1
]
of the imprimitivity Fell bundle
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{B}}\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{op}}
as “adjointable operators with a shift” is also built into our construction, albeit less visibly so.

Even when we consider Fell bundles over groups, Theorem
1.6
can cover some examples that are not covered by
[
1
]
, since there,
X=\mathcal{H}
always. One example is
[
19
, Proposition A.2.3]
; another one is Example
6.9
.

6.3.
Imprimitivity Theorems

Earlier, we alluded to a relationship between Theorem
1.6
and imprimitivity theorems; let is give one example.

Example 6.9

(
[
20
, Example 14]
)
.

If
X
is a locally compact Hausdorff group with closed subgroup
H
, then
H
acts on
X
by right translation (
x\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleleft}}h\coloneqq xh
) and
X
is a principal
H
-space. Its imprimitivity groupoid
X\times_{H}X^{\mathrm{op}}
is isomorphic to the transformation group groupoid
X\ltimes X/H
of
X
acting on the quotient space
X/H
via

(6.3)

X\times_{H}X^{\mathrm{op}}\to X\ltimes X/H,\quad[x,y^{\mathrm{op}}]\mapsto(xy^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}},yH).

In terms of
G\coloneqq X\ltimes X/H
, the
anchor map described in (
1.2
) becomes the quotient map,

\rho\colon X\to G^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle(0)$}}\cong X/H,\quad x\mapsto(1_{X},xH)\triangleeq xH,

and the left action on
X
as described in (
1.3
)
becomes the action

(x,yH)\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\textstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbin{\smalltriangleright}}y\coloneqq xy
.
We conclude that
G

is equivalent to the group
H
via
X
.
To sum up,

\displaystyle X^{\mathrm{op}}{\mskip-2.0mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-5.52933pt\kern 5.91821pt}}_{{\kern-7.83887pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 5.91821pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.8821pt\kern 4.27098pt}}_{{\kern-6.19164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 4.27098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}\ast^{{\kern-3.1821pt\kern 3.57098pt}}_{{\kern-5.49164pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}\kern 3.57098pt{\scriptscriptstyle\rho}}}}{\mskip-2.0mu}X\to H,\quad\mathchoice{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle H}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle H}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle H}}}}{\left\{x^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mid\penalty y\right\}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle H}}}}=x^{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle-1$}}y,\quad\text{ and }\quad X\times X^{\mathrm{op}}\to G,\quad\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle G}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.96312pt{\scriptscriptstyle G}\kern 30.10014pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle X}}}_{{{\scriptscriptstyle G}}}}\left\{x\penalty\mid\penalty y^{\mathrm{op}}\right\}^{{\kern-33.38556pt{\scriptscriptstyle X}\kern 30.10014pt}}_{{\kern-32.96312pt{\scriptscriptstyle G}\kern
